it tuṟavuvēṭam piṟar poruḷik kaḷavu koḷ vataṟkuc cilarkkut tuṇiyāyiṟṟu. kaḷaviṉāl ākiya celvam āvatu pōlat tōṉṟi muṭivil aḷaviṟantu keṭuvatum, kaḷvar tam kaḷḷam veḷippaṭṭavaḻip perun tuṉpam eytuvatum tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār. uṇmit tuṟavikaḷi nōkkiṉār. avar uḷḷattē kaḷavilli; poymmiyum illi. uḷḷattālum poyyā toḻukum avarkaḷi ulakattār uḷḷattil vittup pārāṭṭip paraviṉar. puṟat tūymi nīrāl amivatupōla akattūymi avar vaḻaṅkiya vāymiyāṟ kāṇappaṭṭatu. vāymiyāvatu  yāteṉa nūlkaḷi ārāynta vaḷḷuvar, “vāymiyeṉap paṭuvatu yāteṉiṉ yātoṉṟum tīmiyilāta colal” eṉṟum, “poymmiyum vāymi yiṭattapuritīrnta naṉmi payakkum eṉiṉ” eṉṟum teḷintār. piṟarkkum, “yām meyyāk kaṇṭavaṟṟuḷ illi eṉit
toṉṟum vāymiyiṉalla piṟa” eṉṟu eṭutturittār.
taṉṉalam pēṇātu piṟarkkeṉa muyalum peṟṟi yarākiya tuṟavikaḷ uṭal mutirntu valikuṉṟiyirutta lāl, eḷitil avarkaḷ vekuḷikku iriyāyiṉar. ataṉāl tīmikaḷ pala uṇṭāyiṉa. cellumiṭam cellā viṭaṅkaḷit tērātu, palar ciṉamkoṇṭu iraṇṭiṭattum tuṉpamē uṟṟaṉar. atukaṇṭa tiruvaḷḷuvar ciṉattāl nakiyum uvakiyum aḻivatu nōkkiṉār. avaṟṟiṟkuc ciṉam pakiyāvatum aṟintār. ciṉaṅkoṇṭavartāmē yaṉṟi avar iṉattārum ataṉāl tuṉpuṟṟaṉar. ciṉam koḷḷātāṉ tāṉ karutiyavellām karutiyavāṟu peṟṟu iṉpuṟu talikkaṇṭu, ciṉaṅkoḷvārkkuk kāṭṭiṉār icciṉattāl, palar piṟarkkut tam collālum ceyalālum iṉṉāmi ceytaṉar. atu kaṇṭu varuntiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, it tuṟavikaḷatu aṟivupperumiyi eṇṇiṉār. atu payaṉpaṭāmi kaṇṭu parintār. 
“aṟiviṉāṉ ākuva tuṇṭō piṟitiṉnōy taṉnōy pōṟ pōṟṟāk kaṭi” yeṉṟu aṟivuṟuttiṉār. cilar, iṉṉā
ceytāri vāḷātuviṭiṉ mēlum ataṉiyē ceyvarē yeṉṟaṉar. avarkaṭkut tiruvaḷḷuvar,  “iṉṉā ceytāri yoṟuttal avarnāṇa naṉṉayam ceytuviṭal” eṉṟu teruṭṭiṉār.
potuvāka ulakariyum ciṟappākat tuṟaviyariyum kaṇṭu avaravar uḷḷam, uri, ceyal vakikaḷi aṟintu pōnta tiruvaḷḷuvarkku uṇmit tuṟaviṉkaṇ karuttuc ceṉṟatu. celvam, uṭal, iḷami mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ niliyāmi pulaṉāyiṟṟu. vāḻum vāḻvil ovvoru poḻutum uṭal vāḻvik kuṟittukkoṇṭē varuvati naṉku eṇṇiṉār. uyirkkum uṭampukkum uḷḷa toṭarpu, oru paṟavikkum ataṉ kuṭampikkum uḷḷa toṭarpāka iruttalit teḷintār. uṭaliṉ nīṅkiya uyir eṅkē pōkum eṉa niṉittār. uṭampukkuṭ pōntu otuṅki yiruntu viṭṭup piṉṉar nīṅki yēkum uyirkkup pukumiṭam oṉṟu iruntē tīra vēṇṭumeṉat teḷintār. ivvuṇarvu mutiramutira uṭampiṉkaṇ uḷḷa paṟṟuk kuṟiyat talippaṭṭatu. uṭampiṭamāka iruntu paṟṟiṉi viḷivikkum aimpulaṉkaḷi yaṭakkiṉār. aṭakkātu akappaṟṟiṉippaṟṟi niṟpāri iṭumpikaṉ paṟṟik koṇṭu viṭātiruppati aṟintār paṟṟiṉṟit tuṟantu nōṟkum periyōrkaḷi nōkkiṉār. avarpāl oṉṟiṉpālum paṟṟi llāmi iyalpāy amintukiṭantatu.
paṟṟu ēṉ uṇṭākiṟateṉpatu kāṇaluṟṟār, paṟṟu piṟappukkuk kāraṇamātalik kaṇṭār. piṟap piṟappukkaḷ uḷavātaṟkuriya kāraṇatti aṟiyaluṟṟār. uyirkku uṭampōṭu uṇṭākiya toṭarpukkuk kāraṇam pulaṉāyiṟṟu. eṉṉi kāraṇam ? uṭampōṭu kūṭiyuṟiyum uyirkku noṭitōṟum aṟivu viḷakkam uṇṭākiṟatu; uṭampu aṟivu viḷakkattukkut tuṇiyā vatāl., uyir aṟivu viḷakkam peṟātirunta niliyiruntateṉavum, ataṉip peṟum poruṭṭu vanta uṭampu uyirāl tēṭik
koḷḷap peṟāmiyāl iraṇṭiyum toṭarpupaṭuttu
vōṉ iṟivaṉ eṉṟum, uyirum uṭampum oṉṟi yoṉṟu iṉṟiyamiyā tiruppataṟkē ip paṟṟu uṇṭākiṟateṉṟum, ip paṟṟum aṟivu viḷakkamē ceykiṟateṉṟum, uyir aṟivu muḻuviḷakkam peṟṟa vaḻi uṭampiṉ toṭarpu vēṇṭā teṉṟum, iṟivaṉ viḷaṅkiya aṟivē vuruvāṉavaṉ eṉṟum, ataṉāl avaṟku uṭampu vēṇṭā miyiṉ avaṉpaṟṟaṟṟavaṉ eṉṟum, avaṉ uṇmi yuṇarntu avaṉpāl paṟṟuc ceyyiṉ aṟiviṉ muḻuviḷakkamum ataṉāl uṭampiṉ toṭarpākiya piṟappu uṇṭākā teṉṟum teḷintār. piṟarkkum teḷiya vuṇartta niṉintu, “paṟṟuka paṟṟaṟṟāṉ paṟṟiṉi” eṉṟum, “appaṟṟiṉip paṟṟuka paṟṟu viṭaṟku” eṉṟum aṟivuṟuttiṉār. tuṟavu neṟiyum avarukkuk kikūṭuva tāyiṟṟu.
tuṟaviṉkaṇ niṉṟa tiruvaḷḷuvar, paṟṟaṟṟā ṉākiya meyp poruḷi yuṇarattoṭaṅkiṉār. epporuḷ ettaṉmit tāyiṉum apporuḷiṉ meymmit toṭarpu kāṇpatē aṟiveṉak kaṇṭār. kaṇṭavaḻi, uṭampiṉ vēṟāka uyir viḷaṅkuvatu tērntu, avvuyir uṭampiṟkuḷ iyaṅkum maṉa mutaliyavaṟṟālākiya nuṇṇuṭampiṉ uḷḷē viḷaṅkuvatu aṟintār. uḷḷē iruppatupaṟṟi uyirkku  uḷḷam eṉap peyariṭṭār avvuḷḷam taṉṉiṉ vēṟāka vuḷḷaṉavellām nilipēṟiṉṟi aḻivatāl niliyāka vuḷḷatākiya cemporuḷ uṇmiyiṉik kaṇṭu taṉ aṟivu naṉku viḷaṅkappeṟum; ataṉāl uṭampi vēṇṭātoḻiyum eṉṟu uṇarntu, “ōrttu uḷḷam uḷḷatu uṇariṉ, oru taliyāp pērttuḷḷa vēṇṭā piṟappu” eṉṟār. itaṉi naṉkaṟinta piṟkālattē tōṉṟiya meykaṇṭatēvarum uyiri “uḷḷam” eṉṟē vaḻaṅkiṉār.
cemporuḷ uṇmiyi yaṟiyum aṟivu, ēṉi uṭampu mutaliya cārpukaḷi nōkkac ciṟanta cārpāka iruppatit tiruvaḷḷuvar ciṟappuṟat teḷintu, uṭampum ulakumākiya cārpukaḷ tamakku uṇṭākā vaṇṇam ac cemporuḷ aṟivē cārpāka oḻukiṉār. uṭampōṭu kūṭiyiruntē itaṉic ceytu ciṟantami yāṉ, poruṭcārpukaḷākiya uṭampulakukaḷāl uṇṭākum nōykaḷ tammic cārā toḻivatu kaṇṭār. “cārpuṇarntu cārpukeṭa voḻukiṉ maṟṟaḻittuc cārtārā cārtarum nōy” eṉṟu aṟivuṟuttiṉār.
uṭampōṭu uḷḷavariyum nukarvaṉa nukarntu vāḻa vēṇṭi yiruttalāl, cemporuḷa ṟivupeṟṟu ulakiyaṟ cārpu keṭa oḻukuvatu eṅṅaṉamākum eṉṟoru viṉā avar uḷḷattē eḻuntatu. ataṟku viṭiyāka, itukāṟum uṭampōṭu peṟṟirunta toṭarpāl uyirkku uṇṭākiya avā ataṉit tāṅki
niṟkum uṭampu vaḻiniṉṟu, aṟivi maṟittup piṟappi yuṇṭu
paṇṇu meṉṟum, aḵtuṇṭā kātavāṟu ataṉi aṟut
toḻukavēṇṭum eṉṟum kaṇṭār. ataṉi aṟuttaṟkuriya eḷiyavaḻi, iṟivaṉi vāḻtti vaṇaṅkuvatē eṉat tērntār.
ivvāṟu meyyuṇarntu avā vaṟuttoḻukuṅ kālattil it tirukkuṟaḷi eḻutaluṟṟār. ataṉāl mutaṟkaṇ kaṭavuḷi vāḻttum vāḻttiṉik kūṟiṉār. ataṉkaṇ tāmuṇarnta iṟivaṉi, ātipakavaṉ, vālaṟivaṉ, malarmiciyēkiṉāṉ, vēṇṭutal vēṇṭāmi yillātavaṉ, iṟivaṉ, poṟi
vāyil aintavittāṉ, taṉakku uvamiyillātāṉ, aṟavāḻi antaṇaṉ, eṇkuṇattāṉ eṉṟu viḷakkiṉār. avaṉpāl paṟṟukkoṇṭu, ēṉi uṭampu ulakavāḻvukaḷil paṟṟuvi
yātu vaḻi paṭṭoḻu kiṉāl, uṭampōṭu kūṭiyuṟiyum vāḻvu iṉitu iyalum eṉavum, ārāta iyalpiṉiyuṭiya avāvāl uṇṭākum tuṉpaṅkaḷ illiyām eṉavum, iruviṉiyum vantu aṇiyā eṉavum, maṉattiṉkaṇ kavali mutaliyaṉa illā toḻiyum eṉavum, piṟavit toṭarpu illātu keṭum eṉavum kūṟiṉār.
ivvāṟu vāḻvōr ēṉiyuyirkaḷ iṉpuṟṟu vāḻvatiyē virumpi ataṟkēṟṟa paṇiyiṉic ceyvar. ataṟku avvuyirkaḷāl niṉittapaṭiyē peṟṟukkoḷḷa muṭiyātatu maḻiyātalāl, piṉpu maḻiyik kūṟiṉār. ivaṟṟiyellām ārāyntu kaṇṭurikkum āṟṟa luṭiyār tuṟavuneṟi niṉṟu nītta perumi yuṭiyōr. avar perumiyi maḻi
yiṉiyaṭuttuk kūṟi aṟattip potuvakiyil eṭuttōti valiyuṟuttaruḷiṉār.
ūḻ
aṟamporuḷ iṉpam eṉṟa muttiṟattu vāḻvilum cila nikaḻccikaḷ kāraṇam teriyāmalē uḷavātalit tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār. oṉṟi niṉittuc ceyiṉ, oṉṟēyaṉṟi vēṟoṉṟu viḷivatum, avvoṉṟē viḷiyā toḻiyātoḻivatum, muṟṟilum niṉiyāta toṉṟu vantu nērppaṭuvatum piṟavum tiruvaḷḷuvarkku muṉpirunta cāṉṟōrkkup peruṅ kalakkatti viḷivittaṉa. viṉi, ceyvatu, ceyappaṭu poruḷ,iṭam, kālam, karuvi, nōkkam, payaṉ eṉa eṇvakiyāṉa kāraṇaṅkaḷi oru viṉi nikaḻccikku ētuvākat tolkāppiyar mutaliya cāṉṟōr kaṇṭiruntaṉar. mēlē kūṟiya nikaḻccikaḷ ivvārāyc ciyāl viḷakkam peṟātoḻintaṉa. avi kāraṇam pulappaṭā vakiyil nikaḻntuvarakkaṇṭa paṇṭic cāṉṟōr, avaṟṟiṉ payaṉi nukarntu kaḻittaṉarāyiṉum, pulappaṭāta akkāraṇatti yaṟiyum muyaṟciyil iṭiviṭātu uḻittu vantaṉar.
vāḻvil oruvaṉ nukarum iṉpamum tuṉpamum avaṉ ceyyum viṉi vaḻiyāka uḷavāvaṉa eṉpatu iyalpākat terinta oṉṟu kāraṇam pulappaṭātu nikaḻnta nikaḻcci kaḷālum iṉpamum tuṉpamumē payaṉāy viḷintaṉa. viṉippayaṉākat tōṉṟum iṉpat tuṉpaṅkaḷē, kāraṇam pulappaṭāta nikaḻcci
kaḷiṉ viḷivāyiṉamiyiṉ, iṉpat tuṉpak kāraṇamāṉa viṉiyē, kāraṇam pulappaṭāta vakiyil nikaḻum viḷivu kaṭkum kāraṇamām eṉṟu kaṇṭaṉar. ‘piṟappokkum ellā vuyirkku meṉiṉum, oruvar celvattiṟ piṟantu iṉpuṟutalum, oruvar vaṟumiyiṟ piṟantu tuṉpuṟutalum kāraṇam pulappaṭāta vakiyil nikaḻum nikaḻccikaḷākum; veyiliṟ cellum iruvaruḷ oruvar aḻaṟṟeṟippu (sun- stroke) uṟṟu iṟappatum maṟṟavar iruppatum maḻi nāḷil oru maṉiyiṉ kaṇ irukkum palaruḷ oruvar iṭiyēṟuṇṭu iṟappatum, piṟar iruppatum kāraṇam aṟiyavārāta nikaḻcci kaḷākum. ivvāṟē, paṇṭi nāḷil intiyāvukku vaḻi kāṇpāṉ muyaṉṟa kolampas amerikkāvik kaṇṭatum, in nāḷil rēṭiyam kāṇa muyaṉṟavar pōlōṉiyam kaṇṭatum mēṟkūṟiya nikaḻcciyiṉ pāṟpaṭṭaṉavām. innikaḻcci kaṭkum, ivaṟṟiṉ payaṉi uṟṟōrkkum celavum muyaṟciyum mutaliya viṉi vakiyāl toṭarpuṇṭātaliṉ, pulappaṭāta kāraṇatti viṉi yeṉṟē paṇṭiyōr kūṟiṉar.
oruviṉiyiṉ payaṉākiya iṉpamum tuṉpamum avviṉi muṭivilē perumpālum uḷavākum; cila iṭiyil iṉpam payantu muṭivil vēṟoṉṟu viḷivikkum; cila toṭakkattil tuṉpam payantu muṭivil iṉpam payakkum. ivvāṟṟāl viṉi vakikaṭkēṟpa viṉip payaṉkaḷ mutal iṭi muṭivu eṉṟa viṭaṅkaḷil tōṉṟuvatik kaṇṭu, kāraṇa kāriyaṅkaḷi ārāynta aṟiñar avi oru muṟipaṟṟi nikaḻvatu kaṇṭaṉar.
am muṟiyiṉi ārāynta aṟiñar, viṉip payaṉkaḷākiya iṉpa tuṉpaṅkaḷ, viṉiyiṉkaṇ aruvāyiruntu, avviṉi, viṉi mutal, karuvi, kālam eṉpaṉavaṟṟāl toḻiṟ paṭuṅkāl, tāmum mukiḻttu arumpip pūttuc ciṟappikkum malarpōlciṟantu viṉi muṭiviṉ kaṇ naṟumalar payaṉpaṭuvatu kaṇṭuṇarntaṉar. pūttu maṇañcey ceyali ūḻttal eṉpatu tamiḻ vaḻakku. “iṇarūḻttum nāṟā malaraṉiyar” eṉat tiruvaḷḷuvarum, pūttu maṇaṅkamaḻac caminta arumpiṉi “ūḻmuki” (naṟ. 115) eṉavum, maṇaṅkamaḻntu vayaṅkum pūvi “ūḻuṟupū” (naṟ. 326) eṉavum cāṉṟōrum vaḻaṅkuvatu kāṇka. ivvūḻmukiyum ūḻuṟu pūvumpōla, iṉpamum tuṉpamumākiya payaṉkaḷimaṭṭil payakkum viṉiyum viṉi mutalum karuviyum piṟavumākiya, kāraṇam viḷaṅkāta viṉikaḷi, ūḻviṉiyeṉac cāṉṟōr vaḻaṅkiṉar.
oru viṉikkuriya payaṉ, ataṉ viṉimutalōṭu toṭarpum urimiyum peṟṟu viḷaṅkuvatu kaṇkūṭu kāraṇam pulappaṭāta viṉippayaṉkaḷ oruvari yaṭiyumāyiṉ, avarukkum, ap payaṉkaṭkuk kāraṇamāṉa viṉikkum toṭarpuṇṭeṉpatu teḷivām. payaṉi nōkka, viṉi kālattāl muṟpaṭuvatāl, kāraṇam pulappaṭātu nikaḻum viṉippayaṉkaḷ oruvaruṭiya muṉṉi viṉippayaṉ eṉavum vaḻaṅkappaṭṭaṉa. ivvāṟu, viṉiyuṇarvu tōṉṟik kaṟṟār kallātār eṉṟa irutiṟat tāriṭiyilum naṉku paraviyirunta kālam caṅkakālam. “yām
cey tolviṉik kevaṉ pētuṟṟaṉi”yeṉap pēcum nallantuvaṉār mutaliya cāṉṟōr irunta kālam accaṅka
kālam. atuvē tiruvaḷḷuvar kālamumātaliṉ, ivviṉiyip paṟṟiyum tiruvaḷḷuvar tamatu nūlil kūṟum karuttuṭiyarāṉār aṟam viṉiyiṉmēlum, ēṉip poruḷum iṉpamum viṉippayaṉ mēlum niṟkak kaṇṭu, aṟattukkum poruṭkum iṭiyē ūḻviṉi yeṉap peyariṭṭu, itaṉippaṟṟit tiruvaḷḷuvar pattut tirukkuṟaḷkaḷ vaḻaṅkiṉār.
maṭimiyē vaṭivākavuḷḷa oruvaṉ, oru kāraṇamum paṟṟātu muyaṟciyuṭiyaṉāy oru viṉi ceytu iṉpuṟuvatum, muyaṟci yuruvāya oruvaṉ etirpārā vakiyil maṭiyuṟṟut tuṉpuṟuvatum tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṇṭār; ciṟanta aṟiñaṉoruvaṉ orōvaḻi aṟivu maḻuṅkiyiruntāṉ; piṟitorukāl oruvaṉ piṟa aṟiñar kāṇṭaṟkariya nuṭpatti yuṇarntāṉ; ciṟanta celvamuṭi yārpāl iruttaṟkuriya kūrtta aṟivu, vaṟumit tīyil vataṅku vōṉpāl oḷiviṭṭut tikaḻn tatu; oruvaṉ celvam ceytaṟku ivi nallaṉa eṉat tērntu avaṟṟic ceytāṉāka, avi tīyavāyc celvakkēṭṭiṟkuḷḷāyiṉa; piṟaṉoruvaṉ uṇar viṉṟiyē tīcceyal ceyya, atu mikka celvam payantu avaṟkuc ciṟappi viḷivittatu. miṭiyāl varuntu moruvaṉ miṭimikku ētuvākiya kuṇañ ceyalkaḷiyum iṭam poruḷ kaḷiyum vilakka muyaṉṟāṉ; avaṉ muyaṟci veṟṟitarā toḻintatu. peruñ celva muṭiyavaroruvar nukarakkaṭava nukarporuḷ muṟṟavum nirampap
peṟṟuṇṇum pīṭuṭiyarāyiruntum, nukarātu varuntak kaṇṭār. ivarkaḷ ceyalkaḷellāvaṟṟiṟkum ūḻviṉiyē kāraṇamāvatu uṇarntu nammaṉōraṟiya eṭutturittār. viṉi yuṇarvuṭiyāri nōkki, naṉṟuṇ ṭākuṅkāl ataṉ payaṉākiya iṉpatti ūḻviṉiyeṉa vuṇarntu nukaruvōr tītuṇṭākuṅ kāl atuvēyuṇarntu varuntātoḻital vēṇṭum eṉṟum, ūḻiṉum peruvali yuṭiyatu piṟitu yātum illi yeṉṟum aṟivuṟuttiṉār.
ivvāṟu urittārāyiṉum, ūḻviṉiyē eṉṟu collikkoṇṭu ceyyakkaṭava aṟaṅkaḷiyum, īṭṭaṟkuriya poruḷkaḷiyum nukartaṟkuriya nukarcci kaḷiyum oruvaṉ ceyyā toḻital kūṭātu eṉṟu tiruvaḷḷuvar vaṟpuṟutti yuḷḷār. maṟṟoṉṟu cūḻiṉum tāṉ muntuṟum peru vali
yuṭiya tāyiṉum, iṟivaṉpāl uṇmi yaṉpukoṇṭu avaṉ poruḷcēr pukaḻ purintāri iv vūḻviṉi vantu cērum valiyuṭiyataṉṟu eṉṟaṟku “iruḷcēr iruviṉiyum cērā” eṉṟum, mali tuḷaṅkiṉum kaṭal vetumpiṉum maṉantuḷaṅ kāta vuḷḷamum muyaṟciyumuṭiyārkku ivvūḻ puṟaṅkāṭṭiyōṭum puṉmiyuṭiyateṉṟaṟku, “ūḻiyum uppakkam kāṇpar uliviṉṟit tāḻā tuñaṟṟu pavar” eṉṟum yāppuṟa eṭutturittār.
III. tiruvaḷḷuvarip paṟṟiya katikaḷ
pulavar purāṇam kūṟum kati
tiruvaḷḷuvar, oḷaviyār mutaliya nallōr cilaruṭaṉ piṟantavar. ivaruṭiya tanti pakavaṉ eṉṉum pārppaṉar. tāy ātiyeṉṉum peyariṉar. pakavaṉār piramaṉ “aṅkicattiṉar”; ātiyār kalimakaḷ “aṅkicattiṉar”. ivviruvarukkum makkaḷ eḻuvar piṟantaṉar. avaruḷ āṇ makkaḷ mūvar; avar, vaḷḷuvaṉ, kapilaṉ, cēramāṉ eṉpōrāvar. peṇ makkaḷ nālvar; avar uppi, uṟuvi, oḷavi, vaḷḷi eṉappaṭuvar. im makkaḷ evvevviṭaṅkaḷiṟ piṟantārkaḷō, avvavviṭattilēyē avarkaḷi aṉpiṉṟi viṭṭēkuvatu peṟṟōrākiya pakavaṉum ātiyum mēṟkoṇṭirunta koḷki.
peṟṟōrāl kiviṭappeṟṟu aḻutukoṇṭirunta vaḷḷu vaṉākiya kuḻaviyi, oru vēḷāḷaṉ kaṇṭu ārvattōṭu eṭuttuc ceṉṟu, “ik kaviṉ cēyi in nāḷ eḷitiṉil emakku nalkal mukkaṇāṉ aruḷē” eṉṟu karutip pōṟṟi vaḷarttu vantāṉ. piḷḷi tirumaṇattukkuriya cevviyeytiyapiṉ, avarukku ‘vācuki’ eṉṉum ariya peṇṇoruttiyi maṇam ceytaṉar. ciṉṉāṭkaḷil vēḷāḷaṉum avaṉ maṉiviyum civaṉaṭiyic cērntaṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar tam maṉiviyōṭu kūṭi muṉivarum viyakku māṟu illaṟam purintuvantār. vācukiyum taṉ oḻukkat tāl kaṟpukkelliyāyc cāṉṟōrāl karutappeṟum perum pēṟu peṟṟaṉar. itaṉip pulavar purāṇa muṭiyār,
“	mācaṟu tavattiṉ mikka vaciṭṭamā muṉikku vāytta
 	tēcavir vaṭamīṉ cīrtti ceppuvār ceppun tōṟum
 	vācanāṇ malarpput tēḷām vaḷḷuvaṉ maṉitaṉ māṭci
 	pēcalum ulaki leṉṟum peruvaḻak kāyiṟ ṟammā!”
eṉṟu kūṟukiṉṟār.
ivvāṟu vaḷḷuvar kaṟpuc ciṟanta vācukiyārōṭu vāḻn
tirunta iṭam mayilāppūr. avvūruḷ avar ēṉimakkaḷ vāḻum iṭattil vāḻāmal ūrukkup puṟattē oru kuccuvīṭa mittukkoṇṭu ataṉkaṇ necavut toḻil ceytuvantār. akkālattētāṉ avar tirukkuṟaḷ nūlic ceytār. itaṉōṭu cōtiṭam mutaliya tūya nūlkaḷ cilavaṟṟit tiruvaḷḷuvar ceytāreṉap pulavar purāṇam kūṟukiṟatu.
an nāḷil maturiyil irunta tamiḻc caṅkattup pulavarkaḷ tarukkumikutiyāl, caṅkattiṟku nūleḻuti varum ēṉip pulavarkaḷi ikaḻntu, avartam nūliyum kuṟṟam kūṟi vañcaṉi ceytu vantaṉar. “eṉṉa pāṭṭevarcoṉṉālum itupaḻam pāṭal eṉṉap paṉṉalum eḻutik kāṭṭum paṭiṟum” piṟa tīcceyalum avarkaḷ mēṟkoṇṭiruntaṉarām. vaḷḷuvar ataṉiyaṟiyātu tām eḻutiyaruḷiya tirukkuṟaḷi eṭuttuk
koṇṭu maturit tamiḻc caṅkattil ataṉi araṅkēṟṟak karutic ceṉṟār.
celluṅkāl vaḻiyil orunāḷ perumaḻi poḻintatu. tiruvaḷḷuvar vaḻiyarukē niṉṟa ālamara moṉṟiṉ aṭiyil otuṅkiniṉṟaṉar. aṅkē avarkku muṉṉē oruvaṉ vantiruntāṉ. avaṉ peyar iṭikkāṭaṉ eṉpatu. av viṭikkāṭaṉ, “putta kattup pēyā ciṟṟiliyiṉ kīḻvā”, eṉṟu pēroli ceytu aḻittāṉ. vaḷḷuvarum aṅkē ceṉṟār. ciṟitupōtil maḻiyum niṉṟatu. piṉṉar, avaṉ vaḷḷuvarip pārttu avar varalāṟṟik kēṭṭaṟintu, “uṉ kiyil uṟṟa nūliṟ kūṟum oṇ poruḷ vaki yātu?” eṉṟu viṉaviṉāṉ. avaṟku vaḷḷuvar, “muṟṟa yāviyum moḻintuḷēṉ,” eṉṟu moḻintār. iṭik kāṭaṉ, āṭṭiṉaṅkaḷukku ilitaḻi paṟittutavum kōlik kuṟaḷā
ciriyarkkuk kāṭṭi “itaṟkum pāṭal pāṭiyuḷ ḷāyō?” eṉṟu kēṭṭāṉ. uṭaṉē tiruvaḷḷuvar, “uraṉ eṉṉum tōṭṭi yāṉ ōrintum kāppāṉ, varaṉeṉṉum vippiṟkōrvittu,” eṉṉum pāṭṭiṉi, tam tirukkuṟaḷ nūlilirunta paṭittuk kāṭṭiṉār. atu kēṭṭu, iṭik kāṭaṉ perumakiḻvu koṇṭu, tāṉum tiruvaḷḷu varuṭaṉ maturikkuc cellum karuttuṭiyēṉ eṉṟu colli, avarōṭē maturikkuc ceṉṟāṉ. iruvarum ciṟitaḷavu ceṉṟatum,. vaḻiyil oḷaviyār varakkaṇṭu avarpāl taṅkaḷ karutti veḷiyiṭṭaṉar. avarum tāṉ maturikkuc celvatākat terivittār. piṉpu mūvarum maturi mūtūric ceṉṟu cērntaṉar.
maturiyil caṅkattic cārnta cāṉṟōr cilar immūvariyum nōkki, “nīvir enta ūr?” eṉṟu kēṭṭaṉar. ataṟkut tiruvaḷḷuvar,
“entavū reṉṟīr iruntavūr nīrkēḷīr,
 antavūrc ceyti yaṟiyīrō - antavūr,
 muppāḻum pāḻāy muṭiviloru cūṉiyamāy
 appāḻum pāḻāy aṟum.
eṉṟu viṭiyiṟuttār. 
piṉpu avarkaḷ vaḷḷuvari nōkki, “nīvir yār?” eṉṟu viṉava, avar, “yāṉ vaḷḷuvaṉ,” eṉṟār. eṉṟalum, avarkaḷ caṟṟum matippiṉṟi, “kulikkuntaṉmi koḷḷutikollō?” eṉa ikaḻntaṉar. avvikaḻcciyip poruḷākak koḷḷāta vaḷḷuvaṉār, “kulikkavē vantēṉ,” eṉṟār.
piṉṉar avarkaḷ iṭikkāṭaṉi nōkkiṉar. iṭik kāṭaṉār avarkaḷip pārttu, “āṟṟaṅkari yil irunta māmarattil oru kākkiyiruntu katta, ataṉi ōṭṭum karuttuṭiya iṭicciṟuvaṉ oruvaṉ ataṉi eyyak kōliṉṟik kiyaṟṟu olitta kuṟipput tōṉṟa oru pāṭṭup pāṭumiṉ,” eṉṟār. avarkaḷ, kākki kariyum olik kuṟippum iṭicciṟuvaṉ kiyaṟṟolikkum icik kuṟippum, kuṟikka vaki yaṟiyātu mayaṅkiṉar avar, avarkaḷ cerukkaṭaṅka,
“āṟṟaṅ kariyi ṉarukirunta māmarattil
 kākki yiruntu kaḵkaḵkeṉak - kākkitaṉi
 eyyakkō lillāmal icicic ceṉṟāṉ
 viyakkō ṉārtam makaṉ”
eṉṟa pāṭṭip pāṭik kāṭṭiṉār. ivvāṟē oḷaviyārum pāṭṭukkaḷ pāṭic caṅkappulavariṉ tarukki yaḻittaṉar.
iṉi, caṅkappulavarkaḷ tirukkuṟaḷiṉ cīrmi kāṇṭal vēṇṭit tāmirunta caṅkappalakiyiṉ mēlē ataṉi vittaṉar. appalaki avvoru nūlukkumaṭṭil iṭam tantu appulavar kaḷik kuḷattil vīḻttiṟṟu. atukaṇṭu viyappuṟṟa caṅkap pulavarkaḷ tirukkuṟaḷi viyantu taṉit taṉiyē ovvoru pāṭṭup pāṭi vaḷḷuvari vāḻttiṉar.
maturit tamiḻccaṅkattil tāmeḻutiya tirukkuṟaḷi araṅ kēṟṟic ciṟappeytiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, pāṇṭi nāṭṭavar ceyta ciṟappukkaḷi ēṟṟuk koṇṭu tirumayili vantaṭintu muṉ pōl maṉiyaṟam purintuvantār. atu pōtu tiruvālaṅ kāṭṭil kūttap perumāṉāṉa iṟivaṉ kāḷiyoṭu vātak
kūttu iyaṟṟi mēmpaṭṭāṉ. akkāli aṅkē kūṭiyirunta tēvarkaḷi nōkki avaṉ “emmil veṟṟi yeytiyavaryāvar?” eṉa viṉava, tām aṟiya māṭṭāmi kūṟivaruntiṉar. iṟivaṉār, “nīvir tirumayili ceṉṟu tiruvaḷḷuvarik kaṇṭu teḷintu varuka,” eṉap paṇittār. avarkaḷum makkaḷurukkoṇṭu tiruvaḷḷuvar maṉikkuc ceṉṟaṉar. aṅkē aṟputaṅkaḷ pala naṭantaṉa.
orunāḷ tiruvaḷḷuvar paḻiyatu uṇṇuṅkāl, tam maṉiviyi nōkki, “peṇṇē! itu cuṭukiṟatu, viciṟi koṇarka,” eṉṟār. vācukiyār maṟuttoṉṟum kūṟāmal viciṟi koṇarntu viciṟiṉār. appōtu paḻañcōṟṟil āvi kiḷampiṟṟu. vēṟoru nāḷvācukiyār taṇṇīr mukattaṟkuk kayiṟṟil kuṭamoṉṟik kaṭṭik kiṇaṟṟil iṭṭirukkiyil, tiruvaḷḷuvar avari yaḻittār. maṉiviyār kayiṟṟi aṅṅaṉē viṭṭuviṭṭu vantār. kiṇaṟṟiṟ pātiyaḷavil vantirunta kuṭam avar tirumpi varumaḷavum “antaramāy” niṉṟu koṇṭiruntatu. ic ceyalkaḷik kaṇṭa tēvarkaḷ, vācukiyāriṉ kaṟpunalam kaṇṭu viyantu pārāṭṭit tām vanta karuttit tiruvaḷḷuvarkkut terivittaṉar. avarkaṭku viṭi kūṟaluṟṟa tiruvaḷḷuvar,
“pūvi layaṉum purantaraṉum pūvulakit
 tāvi yaḷantōṉum tāmirukka- nāvil
 iḻinakki nūṉeruṭum ēḻi yaṟivēṉō
 kuḻinakkum piññakaṉṟaṉ kūttu”
eṉṟu moḻintār. tēvarkaḷ ataṉāl maṉantēṟit tam tēvavulaku ceṉṟu cērntaṉar.
ciṟitu kālattukkuppiṉ ēlēlaciṅkaṉ eṉṉum ceṭṭi oruvaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvarik kuruveṉakkoṇṭu aruḷuri peṟṟu uyti peṟa eṇṇi vantāṉ. avaṉi vaḷḷuvar palavakiyāl ārāyn tār. orunāḷ avar avaṉi oru marattil ēṟṟi iru kiyiyum viṭṭu niṟkumāṟu kūṟiṉār. avaṉ “kurupātam tuṇi” eṉa niṉintu avvāṟē ceytāṉ. avaṉ kīḻē viḻavilli. ataṉpiṉ avaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvar maṉiyil vācukiyārkku makaṉpōl oḻuki vantāṉ.
akkālattē nāṭṭil peruvaṟam uṇṭāyiṟṟu. āṉāl, ēlēlaciṅkaṉiṭam kuṉṟupōl celvam kuvin tiruntatu. avaṉip pārttu, “nī vāṅkik kuvittuḷḷa neṟkuviyi vāṅkiyataṟku mēl orupaṭi mikutiyāka viṟpāyāka!” eṉṟār. avaṉum avvāṟē ceytāṉāka, neṟkuvi ciṟitum kuṉṟāmal kuvintē yiruntatu, appōtu, avar “itaṉi vaṟiyārkku variviṉṟi vaḻaṅkuka,” eṉṟār. avvaḻiyum atu kuṟi
paṭāmalē iruppa tāyiṟṟu. piṟitorukāl avar ēlēlaciṅ
kaṉi nōkki, “nī īṭṭiya poṉṉi urukkic caturakkal pōṟ ceytu ataṉmēl uṉ peyarip poṟittuk kaṭalil eṟintu varuka,” eṉak kaṭṭaḷi yiṭṭār. avar colvaḻiyē avaṉum ceytu māṇavaṉ eṉṉum taṉatu uṇmip pāṉmiyi nilināṭṭiṉāṉ.
itu niṟka, ēlēlaciṅkaṉ pulāluṇṇum iyalpiṉaṉ. ataṉi muṟṟavum kiviṭuvateṉpatu avaṉukku aruñ
ceyalāka iruntatu. ataṉiyaṟinta tiruvaḷḷuvar avaṉip paṭippaṭiyākat tiruttak karuti avaṉi nāṭōṟum cettup
pōṉa cēl mīṉ kaḷiyē tiṉṉu māṟu tiṭṭam ceytār. avvāṟē avaṉ ceytuvantāṉ. avaṉukkāka mīṉ valiñar cetta cēl mīṉik koṇarntu tantuvantaṉar. orunāḷ ammīṉ valiñar periyatoru cēlmīṉi valivīcip piṭittuk koṉṟu ataṉ uṭalip piḷantārkaḷ. ataṉ vayiṟṟil pāci mūṭiya poṟkaṭṭi yoṉ ṟiruntatu ataṉi uyarntatoru paccik kalleṉak karutiya mīṉvaliñar  ēlēlaciṅkaṉiṭam tantaṉar. avaṉ ataṉip poṉṉeṉa niṉiyātu taṉ puṟak
kaṭiyil iṭṭāṉ. nāḷaṭivil atu pāci nīṅki muṉṉē avaṉ kaṭalil eṟinta caturap poṟ kallāka iruntatu. atu kaṇṭu viyanta ciṅkaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvarkkut terivittāṉ. avar, “aṟattāṟṟiṟ ceyta poruḷ akaṟṟiṉum akalātu,” eṉac colli avaṉit tēṟṟiṉār.
itu nikaḻnta cilapala nāṭkaḷukkuppiṉ, vācuki yārkku vāḻnāḷ elli kuṟukiṟṟu. avar uyir eḷitil nīṅkātu ūcalāṭuvatāyiṟṟu. atukaṇṭa tiruvaḷḷuvar, “peṇṇē! uṉ maṉattil yātu uḷatu?” eṉṟār. appōtu, vācukiyār, “aṭikaḷē! aṭiyēṉ maṉattil ōr aiyam uḷatu. yāṉ uṇavu paṭikkum pōtellām oru vaḷḷattil taṇṇīrum ōr ūciyum koṇarntu vikkumāṟu paṇittirun tīrkaḷ. yāṉum atu ceytēṉāyiṉum tāṅkaḷ avaṟṟi orunāḷum kiyāṇṭa
tilli. ataṟkuk kāraṇam aṟiya vēṇṭumeṉṟōr avā uṇṭu,” eṉṟār. atukēṭṭu uḷḷaṅkalaṅkiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, cōṟu paṭikkumpōtu cintu māyiṉ, ataṉi ūciyāṟ kōttuk kiṇṇattil kaḻuviyuṇpataṟ kāka avaṟṟi vikkumāṟu paṇittiruntēṉ; nī oru nāḷum cōṟu cintiyatilli,” eṉṟār. ic coṟ kaḷik kēṭṭu maṉavamiti peṟṟa vācukiyārkkuc ciṟitupōtil uyir nīṅkiṟṟu. avar pirivāṟṟāmal tiruvaḷḷuvar,
“	aṭiciṟ kiṉiyāḷi yaṉpuṭi yāḷip
 	paṭicoṟ paḻināṇu vāḷi- aṭivaruṭip
 	piṉtūṅki muṉṉeḻunta pētiyi yāṉpirintāl
 	eṉtūṅkum eṉkaṇ irā”
eṉṟu pāṭi varuntiṉar.
cilanāṭkaḷuppiṉ vaḷḷuvar, ēlēlaciṅkaṉi nōkki, “iṉṉum cilapōtil yāṉ ivvulakiṉiṉṟum nīṅkuvēṉ,” eṉṟu kūṟi, “yāṉ iṟantapiṉ eṉ irukālkaḷiṉum vikkōṟ puriyik kaṭṭi iḻuttuc ceṉṟu, ivvūrkku vaṭakkiluḷḷa kāṭṭil eṟintu viṭuka,” eṉṟu coṉṉār. avar kūṟiyavāṟē cila nāṭkaḷukkuppiṉ avar uyir uṭaliṉ nīṅkic ceṉṟatu. aṅkirun tōr kūṟiyavaṟṟāl maṉam pētuṟṟa ēlēlaciṅkaṉ, avar uṭalit tirumuḻukkāṭṭi oru tērmēl vittu eṭuttēkat talip paṭṭāṉ. uṭaṉē tiruvaḷḷuvar uyirt
teḻuntu, “avvāṟu ceyvatu kūṭātu; yāṉ muṉpu kūṟiyavāṟu ceyvatē vēṇṭuvatu,” eṉa ēlēlaciṅkaṉip paṇittār. atu kēṭṭu naṭukkamuṟṟa ciṅkaṉ oruvāṟu tēṟi avar paṇitta
vaṇṇamē ceytāṉ. avar uṭalit tiṉṟa kākamum kaḻukum viṇṇavarāy vīṭupeṟṟaṉa. avar uṭal tīṇṭappeṟṟavarum uyarmikka tēvarāyiṉar.
ic ceytiyik kaccippatiyil irunta ciṟpiyoruvaṉ aṟintu, avarippōl tiruvuru oṉṟic ceytu tirumayiliyil ākamamuṟippaṭi niṟuviṉāṉ. aḵtu iṉṟum yāvarum kaṇṭu paravip paṇiyumāṟu viḷaṅki varukiṟatu.
tiruvaḷḷuvamāli kuṟikkum kati
tiruvaḷḷuvar maturic caṅkattil caṅkap palakiyēṟit tamatu tirukkuṟaḷi araṅkēṟṟat toṭaṅkiṉār. appōtu, ap palakiyil avarōṭu uṭaṉirukkattakkavar yār eṉṟa aiyam, pulavariṭiyē yuṇṭāyiṟṟu. appōtu vāṉoli (acarīri) tōṉṟi, “teyvat tiruvaḷḷuvarōṭu caṅkap palakik
kaṇ uruttiracaṉmar uṭaṉiruppārāka,” eṉṟatu. piṉpu, uruttira
caṉmar uṭaṉiruppa, vaḷḷuvar tam tirukkuṟaḷi araṅ
kēṟṟiṉār.
caṅkap pulavaruḷ cīttalic cāttaṉār eṉa oruvar iruntār. avar tavaṟuṭip pākkaḷik kēṭka nērum pōtellām tam taliyiṟ kuttikkoḷvatu vaḻakkam. tiruvaḷḷuvar tam tirukkuṟaḷi araṅkēṟṟiyapōtu tāṉ avar taliyiṟ kuttikkoḷḷātu makiḻntu kēṭṭār.
ñāṉāmirta uri kūṟum kati
intiratattaṉ, vararuci, yāḷitattaṉ eṉṟa mūvar, tam viṉittoṭakki velvatu kuṟittu vēṟuvēṟu vakiyiṟ ceṉṟaṉar. avaruḷ yāḷitattaṉ oru caṇṭāḷap peṇṇik kāmuṟṟukkūṭip piṉ avaḷik kiṇaṟṟilē taḷḷiviṭṭāṉ. avaḷi oru pārppaṉaṉ eṭuttucceṉṟu ātarittu vaṭanāṭṭiṟkuk koṇṭu pōṉāṉ. an nāṭṭiṟkuc ceṉṟa yāḷitattaṉ aṅkē appulip peṇṇit taṉakku ēṟṟavaḷ eṉa maṇam ceytu koṇṭāṉ. avaḷ peyar āti yeṉpatu. avaṭku makkaḷ eḻuvar piṟantaṉar. avveḻu varuḷ tiruvaḷḷuvar oruvar.
kapilarakaval kūṟum kati
pakavaṉ eṉpavar ōr aruntavamuṉivar. āti yeṉpavaḷ karuvūr eṉṉum perumpatiyil vāḻnta oru pulicci. ivviru varkkum piṟantavar kapilar.ivaroṭu āṇmakkaḷ mūvarum peṇmakkaḷ nālvarum piṟantaṉar. ivaruḷ uppiyeṉpāḷ ūṟṟuk kāṭeṉṉum ūril vaṇṇār vīṭṭil vaḷarntāḷ.  uṟuvi yeṉpavaḷ maṟṟōr uṭaṉ piṟantāḷ. avaḷ kāvirip pūmpaṭṭiṉattil kaḷviṟpavar cēriyil cāṉṟār oruvar maṉiyil vaḷarntāḷ. peṇmakkaḷuḷ oruvarāṉa oḷaviyār pāṇcēri oṉṟil pāṇar vīṭṭil vaḷarntāḷ. vaḷḷi
yeṉpāḷ kuṟavar vāḻum kuṟicci yoṉṟil vāḻnta kuṟavar kōmāṉ perumaṉiyil vaḷarntāḷ. toṇṭimaṇṭalattut tirumayiliyil paṟiyar maṉiyil vaḷḷuvar vaḷarntār. vañci nakarkkaṇ irunta atikamāṉ illattil atikamāṉ vaḷarntāṉ. tiruvārūril antaṇaṉ oruvaṉ maṉiyil kapilar vaḷarntaṉar.
tirukkuṟaḷ ciṟappuppāyiraṅkaḷ kūṟum kati
pakavaṉuṭiya tanti cōḻanāṭṭu antaṇarkaḷil oruvaṉ. pakavaṉ piṟantatum avaṉ nāṭkuṟippi ārāynta kaṇikaṉ, “pakavaṉ piṟkālattē puṉkulamātu oruttiyik kūṭuvaṉ,” eṉṟaṉar. makaṉukku uṇṭāka irukkum tīviṉikkuk kaḻuvāynāṭi, antaṇaṉ kācikkuppōṉāṉ. patiṉintu āṇṭukaṭkup piṉ pakavaṉ, taṉ tāyāl taṉ tanti vaṭapulam ceṉṟirunta ceyti
kēṭṭut tāṉum piramacariyam pūṇṭu vaṭanāṭu nōkkic ceṉṟāṉ. ceṉṟavaṉ karuvūriyaṭintu ātiyeṉṉum pulimakaḷiṉ peṇmi nalam kaṇṭu kātal mikkukkūṭip piṉ avaḷ pulimakaḷ eṉpatu terintu avaḷik kiviṭṭēkiṉaṉ. āṉāl, ātiyeṉpāḷ avaṉi viṭātu piṉ toṭarn tāḷ. avaṉ uṭaṉē oru kalliyeṭuttu avaḷ neṟṟiyil puṇṇuṇṭāmāṟu eṟintuviṭṭup pōyviṭṭāṉ. ātiyō, avaṉ varukikuṟittu aṟaccāli oṉṟi niṟuvi naṭattivantāḷ.
vaṭapulattil taṉ tantiyip palaviṭaṅkaḷil tēṭiyum kāṇāta pakavaṉ, mīḷat taṉ cōḻanāṭṭukkut tirumpi vantāṉ. vantavaṉ vaḻiyil karuvūril ātiyiṉ aṟaccālikku vantu cērntāṉ. avaṉi āti eṉpavaḷ aṟintu aṭi paṇintāḷ. avaṉ avaḷatu neṟṟivaṭuk kaṇṭu iṉṉāḷeṉa aṟintu muṉ pōl avaḷik kiviṭṭēkak karutiṉāṉ. avaḷ, “iṉi nī nīppiṉ yāṉ uyir vāḻēṉ,” eṉṟāḷ. avaṉ, “īṉṟiṭu makavelām īṉṟuḻi yēviṭut, tūṉṟiya tuṇipoṭu uṭaṉvara valliyēl, varuka,” eṉṟāṉ. avaḷum ataṟkicintu avaṉuṭaṉ ceṉṟāḷ.
iruvarum celluṅkāl vaḻiyil uppi, oḷavi, uṟuvi, vaḷḷi yeṉṟa nāṉku peṇ makkaḷiyum, atikamāṉ, vaḷḷuvar, kapilar eṉṟa mūṉṟu āṇ makkaḷiyum peṟṟuviṭṭu nīṅkiṉar. am makavukaḷ aṅkaṅkē kaṇṭōrāl vaḷarkkap
paṭṭaṉa. vaḷḷuvar mayilāppūril vaḷḷuvar maṉiyil vaḷarntār. piṉpu avar civa tīkki peṟṟuc civaneṟi niṉṟu, pūviciyar marapiṟ piṟanta vācuki yāri maṇantu illaṟampurintu varukiyil tirukkuṟaḷi iyaṟṟiṉār.
ciṉariṭiyil vaḻaṅkum kati
karamuṇṭaṉ eṉṉum viciyaṉukkum cīmatiyeṉṉum avaṉ maṉivikkum makaṉākap piṟantavar ēlāccāri. avarē tirukkuṟaḷi iyaṟṟiyavar. ivar vaṭamoḻiyil pirakiruta moḻiyil pañcāstikāya cāram mutalāka sālammiya pākuṭam īṟāka uḷḷa aimpattiraṇṭu ciṉa nūlkaḷum vaṭamoḻiyil tatvārtta cūttiram, patumananti paccīs eṉṟa iraṇṭu nūlkaḷumtamiḻil tirukkuṟaḷum iyaṟṟiṉār. ivaruṭiya māṇavaruḷ tiruvuḷḷam nayiṉāreṉpavar oruvar. ivarkaḷ tirumayiliyil vāḻntu vantaṉar. ēlāccāriyār tām iyaṟṟiya tirukkuṟaḷi maturic caṅkattil araṅkēṟṟi accaṅkappulavar cerukkaṭaṅki varumāṟu tiruvuḷḷam nayiṉāriṭam tantuviṭuttār. avar ceṉṟu avvāṟē ceytu pōntār. piṉpu ēlāccāriyār vantavācikkaṇmi yiluḷḷa poṉṉūruk karuki
lirukkum nīlakiriyil muṉicaṅkattuṭaṉ iruppārāyiṉar. tiruvuḷḷam nayiṉār araṅkēṟṟiyataṉāl. tamiḻarkaḷ tiruvaḷḷuva nāyaṉār tirukkuṟaḷi iyaṟṟiṉār eṉak kūṟukiṉṟaṉa reṉpar.
kēḷvi vaḻiyāṟ kūṟappaṭum ceytikaḷ
1. paṇṭināḷil pāṇṭināṭṭu maturimā nakaril irunta caṅkappulavarkaḷ civaperumāṉi ikaḻntārāka, ap perumāṉ appulavarkaḷatu cerukkaṭaṅku māṟu piramaṉit tiruvaḷḷu varākavum, tirumāli iṭikkāṭarākavum, kali
makaḷē oḷavi yārakavum makkaḷākat tōṉṟumāṟu paṇittaruḷiṉār.  
2. pakavaṉār peruñcākaraṉ eṉpavarukkum tiruvārūrp pulicci oruttikkum piṟantavar. āti yeṉpavaḷ tavamuṉi yeṉpavarukkum aruṇmaṅki yeṉṉum pārppaṉap peṇṇukkum makaḷākap piṟantavar. piṉpu, avaḷ uṟiyūrp perum paṟiyaṉāl vaḷarkkappaṭṭu mēlūr akarattil nītiyiyar maṉiyil vaḷarntāḷ.
3. pakavaṉ ātiyi mutaṟkaṇ tāṉ kācikkup pōm
pōtu mēlūr akarattil kaṇṭu avaḷatu iḷamiyi eḷḷi aṭittut turattiṉāṉ. piṉpu appakavaṉ kāciyiliruntu tirumpi varuṅkāl avaḷatu peṇmi nalam kaṇṭu kāmuṟṟu maṇañceytu koṇṭāṉ.
4. tiruvaḷḷuvar kuḻavipparuvattil tirumayiliyil ōr iluppi marattaṭiyil peṟṟōrāl kiviṭap peṟṟār. piḷḷipēṟu kuṟittut tavam kiṭanta vēḷāḷaṉ maṉivi yorutti avarik kaṇṭu eṭuttucceṉṟu, tāṉ peṟṟa piḷḷipōl vaḷarttu vantāḷ.
5. “marapaṟiyāk kuḻantiyi vaḷarkkiṟārkaḷ,” eṉṟu ūrār paḻikūṟa, tiruvaḷḷuvari vaḷartta tāy tantiyar avari oru māṭṭut toḻuvil toṭṭiliṭṭukkiṭatti vaḷarttu
vantaṉar. aṟivu vantapiṉ tiruvaḷḷuvar oru paṉimarattaṭiyil iruntu tavam purintaṉar. piṉpu tirumūlar mutaliya cāṉṟō
rōṭu kūṭit tavam ceyvārāyiṉar.
6. kāvirippākkattil mārkkacakāyaṉ eṉṉum vēḷāḷa ṉoruvaṉ āyiram ēr vittup payir ceyyum peruñ celva
muṟṟu viḷaṅkiṉāṉ. avaṉukku vētāḷamoṉṟāl aṭikkaṭi iṭukkaṇ tōṉṟiya vaṇṇam iruntatu. ataṉāl avaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvari yaṭintu kuṟiyirantu niṟka, avar avaṉatu kāvirip pākkattukku vantu vētāḷattiṉ āṟṟalik keṭuttut turattiṉār. atukaṇṭu viyanta mārkkacakāyaṉ taṉ makaḷ vācuki yeṉpāḷit tiruvaḷḷu varukku maṇam ceytu koṭuttāṉ.
vācukiyit tirumaṇam ceytukoḷḷumuṉ tiruvaḷḷuvar vācukiyiṭam maṇalittantu cōṟākac camikkumāṟu paṇittār. avarum avvāṟē camittut tantār. ataṉāl tiruvaḷḷuvar vācukiyi maṇañ ceytu koṇṭār.
7. tiruvaḷḷuvar tirumayiliyil ēlēlaciṅkaṉ eṉṉum vaṇikaṉoruvaṉiṭam nūl peṟṟu neytaṟṟoḻil purintu vantār. annāḷil, tēvarkaḷ, civaṉatu ālaṅkāṭṭu āṭal nalam aṟiyavantu aṟivupeṟṟuc ceṉṟaṉar. ēlēlaciṅkaṉ orunāḷ civapūci ceyyuṅkāl tamatu karuttik kuppattu vāṇikattil celutti yiruntatit tiruvaḷḷuvar kūṟakkēṭṭu avaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvarkku māṇavarāyiṉaṉ.
8. orukāl ēlēlaciṅkar tamakku makkaṭpēṟu vēṇṭu meṉat tiruvaḷḷuvariṭam terivittu varuntiṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar “āṇṭavaṉ aruḷāl aḵtu uṇṭām,” eṉa moḻintaṉar. ciṉṉāṭkaḷil āṇṭavaṉ oru kuḻaviyuruvāy vantu ēlēlaciṅkar muṉ kiṭantaṉar. ēlēlaciṅkarum ataṉit tiruvaḷḷuvarkkuk kāṭṭi ‘aḻakāṉantaṉ’ eṉap peyariṭṭu vaḷarttu vantār.
9. orukāl ēlēlaciṅkaruṭiya kappal karitaṭṭi viṭavē, tiruvaḷḷuvar “ēliyā” eṉṟu colli ataṉi iḻukkumāṟu collit tāmum uṭaṉiruntu iḻuttār. kappal iṉitu kari yaṭintatu.
10. mīṉ valiñar koṇarnta poṟ kaṭṭiyik kalleṉṟu karutiya ēlēlaciṅkar tamatu maṉiyiṉ puṟakkaṭiyil iṭac ceytiruntār. atu pāci nīṅkiyatum poṟkaṭṭiyākat tōṉṟak kaṇṭu vaḷḷuvarkkut teri vittu uṇmi yuṇarntār.
11. annāḷil vāḻnta pulavarkaḷ ceykiyāl neṭuṅ kālamāy maṉattuyaraṭintirunta aḻakāṉantar mutaliyōr vēṇṭukōḷukku icintu tiruvaḷḷuvar tirukkuṟaḷip pāṭi yaruḷiṉar.
12. maturiyil cerukkaṭaṅkiya pulavarkaḷum, ukkirap peruvaḻuti yeṉṉum pāṇṭiyaṉum tiruvaḷḷuvari vaṇaṅkip potiki malikkuc ceṉṟu akattiyarkkut tirukkuṟaḷik kāṭṭumāṟu kēṭṭuk koḷḷavē, tiruvaḷḷuvar tirukkuṟaḷi avarkkuk kāṭṭi avari makiḻvittaṉar.
13. orukāl koṅkaṇareṉṉum cittaroruvar vantu koṇṭirukkiyil, vāṉattiṟ paṟantu ceṉṟa kok koṉṟu avarmēl eccamiṭṭatu. ataṉi avar vekuṇṭu nōkkiṉārāka, akkokku uṭaṉē erintu cāmparāyiṟṟu. piṉṉar accittar vācukiyirunta maṉippuṟattē niṉṟu uṇavu kēṭṭār. appōtu vācukiyār tiruvaḷḷuvarkku uṇavaḷittukkoṇṭiruntamiyāl, puṟattē vantu uṇavaḷittaṟkuc ciṟitu kālamāyiṟṟu. koṅkaṇar ciṉamuṟṟu. vācuki yāri uṟuttu nōkkiṉar. vācukiyār, avar tiṭukkiṭumāṟu, “kokkeṉṟu niṉittaṉiyā, koṅkaṇavā?” eṉṟār. koṅkaṇar naṭunaṭuṅki avaratu kaṟpumāṇpu kaṇṭu vaṇaṅkiṉar.
14. koṅkaṇacittar tiruvaḷḷuvarpāl ñāṉam peṟṟu, illaṟam, tuṟavaṟam eṉṟa iraṇṭaṉuḷ ciṟantatu kāṇa viḻin taṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar avarit tamatu maṉiyiṉkaṇ cilanāḷ taṅkiyirukkumāṟu paṇittār. vācukiyiṉ maṉi māṭcikaṇṭu viyantakoṅkaṇar, “kaṟpuṭiya maṉivi vāykkiṉ, tuṟavaṟat tiṉum illaṟamē ciṟanta,” teṉat tēṟic ceṉṟaṉar.
15. orunāḷ tiruvaḷḷuvar neytaṟṟoḻilic ceytu koṇṭirukkiyil avar kiyilirunta kuḻal kinnaḻuvik kīḻē vīḻntuviṭṭatu. ataṉi eṭuppataṟku viḷakkēṟṟik koṇarumāṟu tiruvaḷḷuvar vācukiyārip paṇittār. avarum avvāṟē ceytār.
16. tiruvaḷḷuvar mācit tiṅkaḷil uttaranāḷil maṇṇu laki nīttaṉar.
tiruvaḷḷuvaratu 
varalāṟṟu ārāycci
1. peṟṟōr 
pulavar purāṇamum kēḷvivaḻic ceytiyum, ciṟappup pāyiraṅkaḷum orumukamākat tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭiya tanti peyar pakavaṉ eṉṟum, tāy peyar āti yeṉṟum kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. ñāṉā mirtameṉṉum nūlurimaṭṭil tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭiya tanti peyar yāḷitattaṉ eṉṟum, tāy ātippulicci yeṉṟum kūṟukiṉṟatu. pulavar purāṇa mutaliyavaṟṟi nōkka, ñāṉāmirta uri paḻamiyāṉatu: āyiṉum, atu kūṟum ceyti nāṭṭil payila vaḻaṅkavilli. mēlum, yāḷitattaṉ kati, ēṉi vararuci, intira tattaṉ mutaliyōr katikaḷōṭu viraviyē ñāṉā
mirtattuṭ kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. ik katikaḷ; kuṇāṭṭiyar eṉpavarāl picāca moḻiyil eḻutappeṟṟa pirukaṟ katiyeṉappaṭum peruṅkatiyil aṭaṅkiya puṉinturikkatikaḷām eṉpatu ataṉ vaṭanūl moḻipeyarppāl viḷakkamuṟukiṉṟatu. aṉṟiyum, ñāṉāmirtam kūṟum, “yāḷi. kūvaṟṟūṇṭum ātip
pulicci kātaṟkācaṉi yāki mētiṉi, iṉṉici yeḻuvarp payantōḷ” eṉṟa aṭikaḷum yāḷitattaṉ ātip pulicciyik kūṭit tiruvaḷḷuvarip payantāṉ eṉṟu kūṟukiṉṟila. ataṉ paḻiyavuriyoṭu kūṭiya ēṭukaḷ “eḻuvareṉṟatu cunantar mutaliya eḻuvari” yeṉṟu kuṟikkiṉṟaṉa. ñāṉāmirta accuppiratiyil kāṇappaṭum uritāṉ “eḻuvar kapilariyuḷḷiṭṭa eḻuvar” eṉṟu urikkiṟatu. itaṟkuriya uri vēṟupāṭu cunantar mutaliya eḻuvareṉpatu, inta ñāṉāmirtat tukkum tiruvaḷḷu varukkum yātōr iyipumilli yeṉṟaṟkup pōtiya cāṉṟākiṟatu.
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭiya peṟṟōr pakavaṉum ātiyumāvar eṉak kūṟum piṟaceytikaṭku ātaravu oṉṟum itukāṟum kiṭittilatu. tirukkuṟaḷil mutaṟkuṟaḷ, ulakiṟku mutal itu veṉṟaṟku “āti pakavaṉ” eṉṟu kūṟuvatu koṇṭu, ātiyeṉpatu tāyatu peyareṉṟum, pakavaṉeṉpatu tantiyatu peyareṉṟum koḷḷap paṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpar cilar ivvāṟu tammip peṟṟa tāytantiyar peyarkaḷik kuṟippākavō veḷippaṭiyākavō kuṟittōtiya tamiḻ nūlāciriyar oruvarum ilar. eṉavē, avvāṟu ōtuvatu tamiḻc cāṉṟōr marapaṉṟu eṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. ataṉāl, tiruvaḷḷuvarip peṟṟa tāy āti yeṉavum, tanti pakavaṉ eṉavum koḷvatu ārāycci yaṟiñar koḷḷat takuvataṉṟām.
iṉi, kapilarakavalum, pulavar purāṇamum, piṟavum oru mukamākak kūṟutalkoṇṭu, tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭiya peṟṟōr pakava ṉārum ātiyārumāvar eṉṟu koḷvatāyiṉ, aḵtu ārāytaṟ kuriyatēyām. tiruvaḷḷuvar kālattē āṭavar ātaṉ eṉṟu peyarpeṟṟu vāḻntatupōla makaḷir ātiyeṉṉum peyaruṭaṉ, “ātimantiyār” eṉṟāṟpōla iruntatuṇṭēyaṉṟi, pakavaṉ eṉṟa peyar tāṅkiyirunta tilli. mukkōlēntit tirinta muṉivarip pakavar eṉa vaḻaṅkiya tuṇṭu. itaṉāl, tiruvaḷḷu varuṭiya tāy peyar ātiyār eṉal poruntumeṉpatum, tanti peyar pakavaṉeṉal iyaṟpeyarāka amiyāteṉpatum viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa.
iṉi, ātiyārip pulicciyeṉavum, pakavaṉip pārppaṉa antaṇareṉavum tiruvaḷḷuvar varalāṟu paṟṟiya kuṟippukkaḷ kūṟak kāṇkiṉṟōm. mukkōlēntit tirinta tuṟavikaḷākiya antaṇar kaḷip pakavareṉpatu paṇṭi nāḷi vaḻakku. ataṉāl, pakavaṉi antaṇareṉṟal oru vāṟu amiyum. aracar, vaṇikar, vēḷāḷar, pārppaṉar eṉpāruḷ yāvarum tuṟavikaḷākiyapōtu antaṇa reṉappaṭuvar. “antaṇareṉpōr aṟavōr” eṉat tiruvaḷḷuvarē kuṟippatu kāṇka. ākavē, pakavaṉākiya antaṉar pārppaṉarākat tāṉ irukkavēṇṭumeṉa varintu kūṟal kuṟṟamām. kapilar mutaliya cāṉṟōrākiya pārppaṉar antaṇarātaliṉ. avarip payantavar pakavaṉ eṉappaṭutaliṉ, pakavaṉi antaṇaṉ eṉap piṟkālattavar karutiyirukkalām. kapilar antaṇareṉap paṭaṟ kēṟpa maṇamum makappēṟu miṉṟit tuṟavu vāḻvu vāḻntavar. pakavaṉākiya pārppaṉarum antaṇarāyiṉ, avar ātiyi maṇantā reṉṟum, tiruvaḷḷuvar mutaliyōrip peṟṟā reṉṟum, aruḷ eṉpatu ciṟitumiṉṟit tām peṟṟa makkaḷi āṅkāṅkuk kiviṭṭēkiṉareṉṟum kūṟappaṭuvaṉa aṉittum poyyurikaḷām.
iṉi, ātiyārip pulicciyeṉpar. tiruttaku teyvat tiruvaḷḷuvarip peṟṟa peṟalarun tirumikka avvammiyāri, “maṅkiyarkkut taṉiyaraci eṅkaḷ teyvam,” eṉa maṉamārap pōṟṟi vaḻipaṭavēṇṭiya kaṭami tamiḻc cāṉṟōrkku uṇṭākiṟatu. avvāṟu ceyyātu, “pulicci”yeṉa iḻittup pēcuvatē vaḻakkātaliṉ, avarukkum tiruvaḷḷuvarkkum toṭarpilli yeṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. nalattiṉkaṇ nāriṉmi
koḷvatu kulattukku mācu ceyvateṉpatit teḷiya
vuṇarntavar  tamiḻc cāṉṟōr.
iṉi, pulāluṇpōr aṉivarum muṟkālattē nūlōrāl puliyareṉappaṭṭaṉar. pulāluṇṇum oruvaṉip puliyaṉ eṉavum, pulāluṇṇum oruttiyip pulicciyeṉavum, vaḻaṅkuvar. “pollāp pulāli nukarum puliyari” (tiruman. 199) eṉat tirumūlar kūṟuvatu kāṇka. paṇṭināḷil pārppār mutaliya palarum pulāl uṇṭaṉa reṉpatu ārāycciyāḷarkkut terinta ceytiyākum. aṅṅaṉamāka, ātiyārimaṭṭil pulicciyeṉṟu kūṟuvatu poyyām. ivvāṟṟāl, ātiyākiya pulicciyit tiruvaḷḷuvar varalāṟṟil iṇitturippatu peruṅ kuṟṟamām.
ivvāṟē, tiruvaḷḷuvarip peṟṟa tāytantiyar tām peṟṟa makkaḷi āṅkāṅkē aṉpiṉṟik kiviṭṭuc ceṉṟaṉareṉa oru kuṟippum kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. avvaṇṇam ceyyakkūṭiya vaṉkaṇmi ettakiya tāyarpālum eḷitiṟ kāṇakkūṭiyataṉṟu; pakavaṉārum ātiyārum koṇṭa poruntā maṇattip puṉintōr, tīneṟiyil makap peṟṟōr ceyyum icceyali ivarkaṇ mēlēṟṟip paṭittu moḻintuḷḷaṉar eṉak koṇṭoḻivatu tavira. itaṉi uṇmiyeṉak koḷḷutaṟku iṭam illi.
2. uṭaṉpiṟantār
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvariyuḷḷiṭṭa eḻuvar ittāy tanti yarkkup piṟanta makkaḷ eṉpatu tiruvaḷḷuvar varalāṟṟiṟ kāṇappaṭum kuṟippākum. avveḻuvariyum nōkkuṅkāl, ikkuṟippip paṭittu moḻintōr ilakkiya aṟivō kālavuṇarvō ciṟitumillāta veḷiṟṟaṟiviṉareṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. oḷavi, vaḷḷi, atikamāṉ, kapilar eṉpōr caṅka ilakkiyaṅ kaḷiṟ kāṇappaṭum cāṉṟōrāvar. ivarkaḷatu varalāṟum ōraḷavu kaṟṟōraṉivarkkum iṉitu terintirukkiṟatu. atu kāṇpōr, ivarkaḷi oru tāy vayiṟṟiṟ piṟantavarkaḷ eṉa orukālum uṭaṉpaṭār.
mēlum, tiruvaḷḷuvaruṭiya matinuṭpattiyum, ulakiyalaṟi viyum nōkkiṉ, ivar oru celvakkuṭiyil piṟantu vaḷarntu centamiḻvaḷam ciṟakkappeṟṟavar eṉpatu teḷivāka viḷaṅkukiṟatu. ellāc cāṉṟōrkaḷum ivarit tiruvaḷḷuvareṉṟē vaḻaṅki yuḷḷaṉar ilakkaṇa vurikārarkaḷ, ‘vaḷḷuvaṉ’ eṉpatu ivaratu iyaṟ peyareṉṟē karutukiṉṟaṉar. ākavē, vaḷḷuvareṉpata ivarkkut tāytantiyariṭṭa iyaṟpeyareṉṟum, ivaruṭiya oppuyarvaṟṟa pulami nalaṅ
kaṇṭu viyappuk koṇṭa cāṉṟōr, tiruvaḷḷuvareṉavum ‘teyvappulavaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvaṉ’ eṉavum pārāṭṭic ciṟap pippārāyiṉa reṉṟu aṟital vēṇṭum.
3. ūr
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvaṉāratu ūr tirumayili (mayilāppūr) eṉpatu perumpāṉmiyāṉa vaḻakku. ciṉa camayattavar iṭiyil vaḻaṅkum katiyum tirumayiliyik kuṟikkiṟatu; tiruvaḷḷuva māliyoṉṟumē ivaratu iṭam maturiyeṉak kuṟikkiṉṟatu. itaṟkēṟpavē, tiruvaḷḷuvar nūṟku urikaṇṭa parimēlaḻakar toṇṭināṭṭiṉareṉak kaṇṭa toṇṭi maṇṭala catakam, “tirukkāñcivāḻ parimēlaḻakaṉ vaḷḷuvaṉārkku vaḻikāṭṭiṉāṉ toṇṭi maṇṭalamē” (41) eṉṟu kūṟi, vaḷḷuvaṉārkkum iṭam ittoṇṭināṭē eṉa eṭuttōtu
kiṉṟilatu. tirumayili toṇṭināṭṭiṉ kaṇṇatōrūr eṉpati,
“kayiliyuṅ* kacciyum viṟkōla mumnaṟ kaḻukkuṉṟamum
 payilvaḻi tāyamum pācūru macciṟu pākkamunal
 layiliyum vallan tiruvoṟṟi yūru maraciliyum
 mayiliyum īcaṉ makiḻntataṉṟō toṇṭi maṇṭalamē.”
eṉṟu kuṟittōtukiṟatu. tirumayiliyil uḷḷa tiruvaḷḷuvar kōyilum piṟkālattē kaṭṭappeṟṟa teṉpati muṉpē kūṟiṉōm. ivvāṟṟāl, tiruvaḷḷuvarkku ūr tirumayili eṉakkūṟuvatu oru taliyākat tuṇitaṟ killāmiyum, eṉavē, tiruvaḷḷuva māli kūṟuvatē valiyuṟuvatum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
4. maṉi vāḻvu
iṉi, tiruvaḷḷuvar vācuki yeṉpavari maṇantu illaṟam purintuvantaṉareṉṟa ceyti, mēṟkūṟiya kēḷvivaḻi varalāṟṟilum ēṉip purāṇa mutaliya vaṟṟilum oppak kūṟappaṭukiṟatu ātiyār pakavaṉār eṉṉum peyarkaḷi nōkka. vācuki yeṉṉum peyar ārāyccikkuriya toṉṟākiṟatu. vācuki yeṉṉum vaṭamoḻip peyar, annāḷil tamiḻnāṭṭil vaḻaṅkiya tamiḻar peyarākat tōṉṟavilli. vaṭavarum, avaratu vaṭamoḻiyum viraviyirunta kālattē tiruvaḷḷuvar vāḻntārāyiṉum vaṭamoḻip peyaruḷ vācuki yeṉpatu pōlum peyar makkaṭku vaḻaṅkiyatilli. tammi vaṭavarākak karuti yoḻukum innāḷip pārppaṉarum tām tamakku vaṭamoḻip peyariyiṭṭu vaḻaṅkiya vaḻakkam. nāyakka maṉṉar āṭciyiṟṟāṉ perukiṟṟeṉpatip pallāyirak kaṇakkāka eṭukkappaṭṭirukkum teṉṉāṭṭuk kal veṭṭukkaḷ eṭuttōtukiṉṟaṉa pārppaṉarum ki.pi. patiṉintu patiṉāṟām nūṟṟāṇṭuvariyil vaṭamoḻip peyarip peritum tamakku iṭṭu vaḻaṅkātiruntaṉarāka. pārppaṉarallātārākiya tiruvaḷḷu varum piṟarum tam makaḷirkku vaṭamoḻip peyariṭṭu vaḻaṅkiṉareṉak kūṟuvatu uṇmiyuriyākātu. ākavē, tiruvaḷḷuvarkku maṉivi yār oruvar iruntaṉareṉpatu uṇmiyāyiṉum, avar peyar vācuki yeṉa vaḻaṅkiṟṟeṉpatu porutta maṉṟeṉṟu kāṇ kiṉṟōm ivvāṟē, vācukiyār tanti peyar mārkkacakāya mutaliyāreṉpatum muḻutta poyyuriyākiṟatu. kāvirippākkat tukku mēṟkil pālāṟṟiṉ kariyil irukkum viriñci purattuk kōyililuḷḷa iṟivaṉi aṅkuḷḷa paḻaṅ kalveṭṭukkaḷ vaḻittuṇi nāyaṉā reṉṟum, piṟkālak kalveṭṭukkaḷ mārkkacakāyīccura reṉṟum kuṟikkiṉṟaṉa. mārkkacakāyaṉeṉṟa iṟivaṉ peyar nāṭṭil vaḻaṅkiya piṟkālattē makkaḷ tamakkum mārkkacakāyaṉeṉap peyar koṇṭiruntaṉarātalāl, ap peyaruṭiyār oruvar, tiruvaḷḷuvar kālattē iruntā reṉpatu poruntātu. kāvirippākkattiṉ paḻam peyar kāvitippākkam eṉpatiyum īṇṭu niṉivukūrtal vēṇṭum.  itaṉāl mārkka cakāyar varalāṟum piṟavum avar peyarpōlap piṟkālat teḻunta puṉinturiyeṉak koḷvatē poruttam.
tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṟpuṭiya maṉiviyāruṭaṉ kūṭi vāḻntu varukiyil necavuttoḻil purintuvantāreṉṟu kūṟi, “iḻi
nakki nūl neruṭum ēḻiyaṟivēṉō,” eṉa avar tammik kūṟuvatākac collum veṇpāvi ataṟkuc cāṉṟākak kāṭṭuvatu uṇṭu. āṉāl, attoḻil paṟṟiya kuṟippoṉṟum avaratu nūliṟ kāṇappaṭavilli. uḻavu, vāṇipam, araciyal, amicciyal, tūtupōtal, pōr ceytal, maṟiyōtutal mutaliya toḻilkaḷi āṅkāṅkuk kūṟuvatupōlavēṉum tamatu neytaṟṟoḻili avar kuṟittilar. ātalāl, avar neytaṟṟoḻilittāṉ tamatu toḻilākak koṇṭi runtāreṉak kūṟutaṟku iṭamilli.
tiruvaḷḷuvar kaṟpuṟu poṟpumikka maṉivi yāroṭu kūṭi  illiruntu nallaṟam purintu varukiyil, tamiḻ nāṭum tamiḻ makkaḷum uytal vēṇṭi, tām tam nallaṟivukoṇṭu ārāynta naṉṉūṟ poruḷ aṉittiyum naṉku teḷintu ulaka vāḻvil avvappōtu kaṇṭa ariya kāṭcikaḷiyum tērntu, yāvarkkum oppap payaṉpaṭattakka nūliyaṟṟa vēṇṭumeṉṟa karuttāl untappeṟṟut teyvat tirukkuṟaḷ nūlic ceytārātal vēṇṭum.
5. nūliyaṟṟal
ivar kālattē makkaḷ kūṭṭattilkaṭavuḷ uṇmiyum, viṉittoṭarpum, iṟivaṉ tiruvaṭi yaṭivatē vīṭupē
ṟeṉṉum vīṭupēṟṟuṇarvum tuṟakka narakaṅkaḷ uṇṭeṉṉum koḷkiyum piṟavum nilaviṉa. vētiyar maṟiyum tuṟaviyar tuṟavu neṟiyum viraviyiruntamiyiṉ, ivarkālam, vaṭavaratu vētaneṟiyum tamiḻaratu civaneṟiyum kalanta tiruneṟi oru
maruṅkum, putta camaṇarkaḷiṉ putuc camaya neṟi orumaruṅkum nilaviya kālamākum. iraṇṭaṉpayiṟciyāl makkaḷ kūṭṭam ulakiyalil cīriya nāṭṭam peṟātu cīrkulivatāyiṟṟu. vitika
neṟiyiṉar, piṟappil uyarvu tāḻvum, ulakiyalil niliyāmik kuṟippum, ulaka vāḻvil veṟuppum piṟavum kaṟpittu, makkaḷi ulakiyaṟporuḷi ārāytaṟkum, makkaḷiṭiyē orumiyuṇarvu tōṟṟuvittu oṟṟumi yoṭu vāḻtaṟkum ūkkaṅkoḷḷātu vēṟuvēṟākap pirintu keṭutaṟku ulivit toḻukiṉar. putta camaṇarkaḷ, pollā nōṉpum vāḻkkiyiṟ paṟṟiṉmiyum peruṅ koḷki kaḷāka eṭuttōti, araciyal, camutāyam, poruḷīṭṭal mutaliya tuṟikaḷil makkaḷ karuttu īṭupaṭāta niliyiṉiyuṇṭu paṇṇiṉar. itaṉviḷivē kaḷappirarum pallavarum piṟarum nāṭṭil nuḻintu araciyalik kippaṟṟit tamiḻaratu toṉmi varavu keṭuttaṟku iṭam ceytatu eṉiṉ atu tavaṟākātu.
paṟṟukkoṇṭu ceyyum ilvāḻvu tuṉpattukku ētu; ‘puttēḷulakam putalvarum tārār,” eṉpatu puttarkaḷiṉ poru ḷuriyākum.
ittakiya koḷkikaḷikkēṭṭa teṉṉāṭṭavar, ivaṟṟi mēṟkoṇṭu orumi nōkkam peṟātu talitaṭu māṟalāyiṉar. putumikku aṭimiyākum puṉmi tamiḻ makkaḷ
pāl iruppatukaṇṭu maṉam varuntiya tiruvaḷḷuvar, ulakiyaṟkup poruḷāvaṉa aṟamum poruḷum iṉpamum eṉap paṇṭiyōr uritta uṟutip poruḷkaḷiyē mīḷavum eṭuttut tokuttum vakuttum virittum urikka vēṇṭiya kaṭami aṟivuṭiya cāṉṟōrkku uḷatāvati naṉku uṇarntār. ulakiyalil ceyyum nalvāḻvē vīṭupēṟṟukku vāyilām eṉpati vaṟpuṟutta viḻintār; viyattuḷ vāḻvāṅku vāḻvatu vāṉuṟiyum teyva vāḻvām eṉpatu paḻantamiḻaratu vāḻkkik kuṟikkōḷ. itaṉiyē pallāṟṟāṉum ettiṟattōrum viḻintēṟkum vaṇṇam nūl ceyvārāyiṉar. ivvuṇmi
yiṉi naṉku ārāyntu kaṇṭē kallāṭaṉār eṉpār, “camayak kaṇakkar mativaḻi kūṟātu, ulakiyal kūṟip poruḷitu veṉṟa vaḷḷuvaṉ,” eṉṟu viḷakkiṉār. ēṉiyōrellām ivvulakiyalik karuvi yākakkoṇṭu tattam camayak karuttiṉiyē vaṟpuṟutta muyaṉṟaṉar.
ulakiyali, aṟam, poruḷ, iṉpam eṉṟa mūṉṟu kuṟik kōḷiṉkaṇ vittu ārāynta mutaṉmi tolkāppiyark kurittu. ataṉip payaṉpaṭavuṇarntu uṇmiyuṇarvāl makkaṭku vakuttum virittum uritta uyarnilipperumi tiruvaḷḷuvark kēyuriyatu. ivarpōl ulakiyaṟporuḷi ivvaṇṇam vakuttum virittum uritta aṟiñar, ipparanta   maṇṇulakil evarumē ilar eṉpati yāvarum oppavurik
kiṉṟaṉar.
6. tirukkuṟaḷ taṉittamiḻ mutaṉūl
iṉi, ulakil kāṇappaṭum nūlkaḷuḷ mikavum paḻami
yāṉatu vētam eṉpar. “ulakiyal vētanūl oḻukkam” eṉṟaṉar cēkkiḻār. atuvum ulakiyalik kaṉmamum ñāṉamum eṉa iraṇṭāka vakuttōtuvatākak kūṟuvar āṉāl, ulakiyalil uyirkaḷ ceytaṟkuriyatu aṟam: ataṉāl ceyyappaṭuvatu poruḷ; ataṉāl avvuyirkaṭ kuḷatākum payaṉ iṉpam eṉṟu vakuttōtum aṟivuṭi yiyalpu vētattukkum kiṭiyātu eṉpar.
ittirukkuṟaḷ nūl amintuḷḷa muṟi vēṟē em moḻiyiṉum kāṇappaṭavilli; ākavē, itaṉ amipput tiruvaḷḷuvar tāmē paṭittukkoṇṭa toru taṉitta uṇmi muṟiyākum. nilavulakil aṟivuṭi makkaḷ kāṇum uyirk koḷki palavum itaṉkaṭ kāṇappaṭum;āṉāl, ittirukkuṟaḷiṉ kaṇṇuḷḷa koḷkikaḷ pala piṟa ennāṭṭu emmoḻi nūlilum kāṇappaṭuvatilli. ituvē itaṟkuḷḷa taṉimi nalamum perumi nalamumākum.
iṉi, tamiḻnāṭṭut tamiḻmakkaḷuṭiya taṉi nikaḻcci varalāṟukaṭkum, uṇmikaṭkum, tamiḻar ūrkaṭkum taṉimiyum toṉmiyum uṇṭeṉpatu aṟiyātu, vaṭavarnāṭṭu vaṭavar vaṭamoḻikkaṇ toṭakkum tōṟṟamum nāṭi alamarum kīḻmi iṭikkālattē eṅṅaṉamō uṇṭāyiṟṟu. tamiḻ
nāṭṭu mūtūr kaḷāṉa maturi, tilli. tiruvaṇṇāmali, tiruvēṅkaṭam, tirukkāḷatti mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ toṉmi varalāṟum, tamiḻnāṭṭut tamiḻ makkaḷ nikaḻcci varalāṟukaḷāṉa cilappatikāram, tiruntoṇṭar purāṇam mutaliyavaṟṟukkum vaṭamoḻiyil toṭakkamum tōṟṟu vāyum kaṇṭurippavar uḷarāyiṉar ‘aracamarattip piṭitta caṉi, ataṉ kīḻ irunta aiṅkaraṉiyum piṭitta teṉpatu pōla, mikap paḻantamiḻaratu toṉṉūlkaḷāṉa tirukkuṟaḷukkum tolkāppiyat tukkum “vaṭamoḻi mūlam” kaṇṭu, ilatākiya taṉāl poy yēṉum puṉintu kūṟum puṉmi nāṭṭil uṇṭāki viṭṭatu. ataṉāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭōr palar, tiruvaḷḷuvar mutalāyi ṉōrkkup poy varalāṟu puṉintatōṭu nillātu, tirukkuṟaḷ partturuhari, nīticatakam, pakavaṟ kīti, maṉumiruti, kuṭiliyar aruttacāttiram, cukkiranīti, kāmantakam mutaliya vaṭa nūlkaḷiṉ moḻi peyarppeṉṟum kūṟuvar.
tamiḻ vāḻviṉ taṉippayaṉāyt tōṉṟit tamiḻp pulami ciṟantu tamiḻ niṉivu tamiḻc ceyalkaḷit tamiḻuḷḷattiṟ kaṇṭuritta tamiḻt taṉimakaṉārāṉa tiruvaḷḷuvar, taṅkālattu vaḻakkum ceyyuḷum ākiya iru perun tamiḻk kaṭalkaḷiyum nuṇmāṇpulami yeṉṉum mattiṉik koṇṭukaṭin
teṭutta taṉipperuntamiḻamutam it taṇṭamiḻt  tirukkuṟaḷ eṉpatu yāvarum uḷḷakkiḻiyil uruvupeṟap poṟittuk koḷḷa vēṇṭiya uṇmiyākum.
tirukkuṟaḷ eṉṉum ariya aṟanūl ulakiyalukkuriya peru nūlākum. ulakiyalil kūṟutaṟkuriya periya poruḷkaḷ pala vaṟṟiṉum, aṟam, poruḷ, iṉpam eṉṟa mūṉṟiṉum perumi mikka poruḷ vēṟē kiṭiyātu. vīṭupēṟum mūṉṟaṉ ētuvākap peṟappaṭuvatēyaṉṟi, immūṉṟāṉumē eytap paṭuvatumākaliṉ, perumi yeṉpatu immūṉṟukkumē uriyateṉpatu tēṟṟam. ākavē, evvakiyiṉum aṟa mutaliya mūṉṟumē perumikku urimi koṇṭu niṟṟaliṉ am mūṉṟaṉiyum viḷakkum nūlatu perumikku vēṟē emmoḻi ennūlum iṇiyākātākaliṉ, ituvē ellāpperumikkum urittāyp perunūl eṉappaṭu vatāyiṟṟu.
perunūlkaḷi yāttaṟkup perumi mikka vaḻikaḷ pala uṇṭu. perumiyip peritāyk kāṭṭuvati viṭa, ap perumi nalam ciṟitum kuṉṟātavakiyil ciṟumikkaṇ aṭakkip palarkkum teriyappaṇ ṇuvatu tāṉ pulavaratu cīrtta pulamic ceyalākum. mēlum perumip poruḷip peruneṟipaṟṟi uṇarttaluṟiṉ,.  apperuneṟi tēṟi nuṉittuṇarum aṟivup perumiyuṭiyārkkē an nūl uriyatāyviṭum. nūṟporuḷ, periyōr ciṟiyōr aṉivōrkkum potu
vātalāl, ciṟumiyaṟiviṉarāṉa makkaḷ palarum aṟitaṟkākavē, pākkaḷuṭ ciṟukiyatum kuṟukiyatumāṉa kuṟaṭpākkaḷāl innūli āciriyar tiruvaḷḷuvar yāttirukkiṉṟār.
7. nūṟpeyar
ivvāṟu, kuṟumpāvākiya kuṟaṭpāvāl, perum poruḷi yuṇartta vantavar, kuṟaḷ eṉṟē nūlukkum peyar kuṟittiruk kiṉṟaṉar. nūṟpeyar viḷaṅkap peyar kuṟittilar. ulakil nilavum perumporuḷi yellām oruvaḻiyāl iṉitu eḷitu viḷaṅkak kūṟuvatāl, nūṟporuḷi yuḷḷaṭakkiya potuppeyaroṉṟāl cuṭṭikkūṟal amiyāmipaṟṟi, karuviyākiya kuṟaṭpāviṉ peyarāl nūlik kuṟittiruppatu nōkkattakkatu. itaṉkaṭ kūṟappaṭum aṟa mutaliya poruḷkaḷ oṉṟaṟkoṉṟu ētuvum payaṉumāy iyintu pirippaṟa niṟṟalāl, immūṉṟiyum tokuttuc cuṭṭavallatoru potuppeyar itaṟku eytātāyiṟṟu. aṟanūl eṉṟu peyar kuṟittāl, atu poruḷiyum iṉpattiyum coṉṉa māttirattē kēṭṭār maṉattuṭ kiḷarntu eḻuvikkum vaṉmiyuṭiyatā kātu; ēṉipporuṇūl iṉpanūl eṉṟu kuṟitta vaḻiyum atuvē muṭivākum. iṉi oḻukkanūl eṉṟa vaḻi. atu, vaḻakku, taṇṭam eṉpaṉavaṟṟiyum cuṭṭātākiṉṟatu; in nūṟkaṇ, vaḻakkum taṇṭamum āṅkāṅkup potuvakiyil kūṟappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. ulakiyal nūl eṉṟakkāl. ataṉōṭiyittuk kāṇappaṭum aṟivunūl eṉpataṉiṉ nīṅkit taṉimiyuṟum; iḵtu aṟivu
nūlumātaliṉ, ciṟappuṭiya oṉṟaṉ peyarāl varintu kūṟuvatum kuṉṟak kūṟalākiṉṟatu. ivvāṟu ellāmāy allatumāy uḷḷa poruḷvaki ellāvaṟṟiyum cuṭṭivarum innūṟkup poruḷiyalpu kāṭṭum peyar oṉṟu kiṭittal aritātal kaṇṭē, āciriyar, kuṟaṭpāvākiya karuviyaṉālēyē kuṟaḷ eṉṉum peyarāl vaḻaṅkiṉareṉak koḷḷalvēṇṭum tiruvaḷḷuvaratu teyvap pulamiyiṉ viḷivākat tōṉṟiya ciṟappuppaṟṟic cāṉṟōr ikkuṟaQ lit tirukkuṟaḷ eṉṟu pārāṭṭu vārāyiṉar.
iṉi, it tirukkuṟaḷi aṟattuppāl. poruṭpāl, iṉpattuppāl (kāmattuppāl) eṉṟu mūvakippaṭuttu, ovvoṉṟiṉiyum cila pala atikāraṅkaḷāka vakuttu atikāram ovvoṉṟiṉiyum pappattuk kuṟaṭpākkaḷāl kūṟiccelkiṉṟār. pappattāka niṟkum kuṟaḷkaḷum, eṭuttuk koṇṭa poruṭku ilakkaṇam kūṟuvatum, ētuvum payaṉum kūṟuvatum, poruḷiyal purippatum, vitippatum, maṟuppatum, cila poruḷkaḷit tuṇi yeṉpatum, cilavaṟṟi aṇiyeṉpatum, cilavaṟṟip paṭi yeṉpatum, muṟi ceyvatum, muṉpiṉ nōkki valiyuṟuttu vatum, nūlōr muṭipeṉpatum, cāṉṟōr mēlvittuk kōṭaṟ pāṉmi kāṭṭuvatum, uvami vakiyāṟ cilavaṟṟi viḷakkaluṟṟu, poruḷiyē uvamam ceyvatum, ēṉiyavaṟṟi uvamam ceyvatum, urupu virittu uvamippatum, uvamiyum poruḷum oruṅku vittu uruvakam ceyvatum, oṭṭu, vañcap pukaḻcci mutaliya aṇiyuṟak kūṟuvatum,  viyappu, vekuḷi mutaliya meyp pāṭu tōṉṟak kūṟuvatumākiya pala vakiyāl iyaṉṟuḷḷaṉa.
iṉi, aṟattuppāluḷ aṭaṅkiya atikāraṅkaḷuṭ cila
vaṟṟit tokuttup pāyirameṉavum, illaṟaviyal, tuṟaviṟaviyal eṉavum, poruṭpāl atikāraṅkaḷi vakuttu, araciyal, aṅkaviyal, oḻipiyal eṉavum, iṉpattuppāliṟ kāṇappaṭum atikāraṅkaḷi, kaḷaviyal kaṟpiyal eṉavum vakuttaṭakkik kūṟiṉar eṉpar. ivviyal vakuppukkaḷ oru neṟiyāka vaṉṟi vēṟu vēṟu vakiyāka vakuttuk kūṟap paṭṭuḷḷaṉa eṉat tiruvaḷḷuvamāli terivikkiṟatu. maṇakkuṭavar uriyil ivviyal vakuppu vēṟupaṭukiṟatu. itaṉāl, iyal vakuppu
maṭṭil āciriyar tiruvaḷḷuvaṉār koṇṭataṉṟu eṉpatu teḷiya viḷaṅkukiṟatu. atikāraṅ kaḷiṉ vippumuṟi maṭṭil, parimē laḻakar, maṇakkuṭavar mutaliyōratu tokuppil vēṟupāṭiṉṟi oppa nilavuvatāl, ammuṟi, āciriyarē kaṇṭurittateṉak koḷḷalām.
aṟivu, viḻivu, ceyal eṉṟa mūṉṟum uyirkaṭkuriya paṇpukaḷākum. avaṟṟuḷ aṟivu poruṭ pēṟṟukkum, viḻivu iṉpa nukarccikkum ceyal aṟañceytaṟkum payaṉākum. ippaṇpum payaṉum koṇṭu iṉpamē nāṭiniṟpatupaṟṟi, poruḷatikārattu iṉpattiṇiyiṉ iyalpu kūṟappukunta tolkāppiyar “iṉpamum poruḷum aṟaṉum” eṉa muṟi ceytu kūṟiṉār. aṟattāṉ varuvatē iṉpam; poruḷilārkku ivvulakil iṉpamilli eṉa aṟamum poruḷum iṉpap pēṟṟukku ētuvātalit tiru vaḷḷuvar ceyalmuṟiyiṟ kūṟukiṉṟār; ataṉāl aṟattuppāli mutaṟkaṇṇum, poruṭpāli iṭiyilum, iṉpattuppāli muṭivilum muṟi ceytu
vittu muppālākiya tirukkuṟaḷi urittuḷḷār. iṉi, parimēlaḻakar, immi maṟumi vīṭu eṉṟa mūṉṟiyum payappatu
paṟṟi aṟam mutalilum, immi maṟumi eṉṟa iraṇṭiyum payappatupaṟṟip poruḷ iṭiyilum immi yoṉṟē payappatupaṟṟi iṉpam iṟutiyilum vikkap paṭṭaṉa eṉpar.
8. araṅkēṟṟam
iṉi, it tirukkuṟaḷ nūl maturiyil irunta caṅkattil araṅkēṟṟappaṭṭateṉṟum, akkālattē innūli ēṟka maṟutta caṅkappulavar, ittirukkuṟaṭku iṭantarumukattāl caṅkap
palakiyāl teruṭṭap paṭṭaṉa reṉṟum kūṟappaṭum ceytiyum piṟkālattōr puṉintu viṭṭa poyyuriyē. nallataṉ nalatti uḷḷavāṟu kāṇum uravōrē pulavareṉap paṭuvarāki yāl, avar kūṭiya pēraviyil tiruvaḷḷuvar mutaṟkaṇ puṟakkaṇikkap paṭṭāreṉṟaṟku accaṅkac cāṉṟōr ceytuḷḷa pāṭṭukkaḷiṉ tokutikaḷākak kāṇappaṭum toki nūlkaḷ ātaravu taruvaṉavāka illi.
9. iṟutinili
muppāl vaḻaṅki muḻutulakum toḻutēttum mūvāp pukaḻpeṟṟa mutaṟ pulavarāṉa tiruvaḷḷuvaṉār, piṉpu, ēlēlaciṅkar eṉṉum celvar toṭarpu peṟṟu maṉiyaṟam purintuvantaṉar eṉṟa ceytipotuvākak kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. avaṉ ivarpāl māṇavaṉāy aṟaṅkēṭṭāṉ eṉpārum, ivar avaṉpāl nūlpeṟṟu necavuttoḻil purintār eṉpārum pala tiṟattar uḷar. ivi ettuṇi yuṇmiyāṉavi eṉpatu aṟiyak
kūṭāta tāyiṉum, iruvarkkum toṭarpu iruntateṉak koḷvatil kuṟṟamilli tiruvaḷḷuvarkku makkaṭpēṟu uṇṭō illiyō eṉṟu kūṟattakka vakiyil avar varalāṟu viḷakka miṉṟiyuḷatu. tirukkuṟaḷi nōkkumiṭattu avarkku makkaṭpēṟu uṇṭeṉat teḷitaṟku iṭamirukkiṟatu. avarkaḷ tiruvaḷḷuvarpōl poṉ ṟāppukaḻ peṟum pēṟu ilarāka irukkalām.
tiruvaḷḷuvar viṇpukuvataṟku muṉpē avar maṉiviyār tam pūtavuṭampu nīttaṉareṉa avar varalāṟu kūṟukiṟatu. avar pirivāṟṟātu vaḷḷuvar varuntip pāṭiyatākap pāṭṭoṉṟum kāṭṭappaṭukiṟatu. ciṉṉāḷ kaḻittut tiruvaḷḷuvarum iṟivaṉ tiruvaṭi cērntā reṉṟum, avaruṭiya māṇavarākiya ēlēla ciṅkarē iṟutikkaṭaṉ āṟṟiṉāreṉṟum kūṟukiṉṟaṉar. yātōr āṭamparamumiṉṟi mikka eḷiya niliyil tiruvaḷḷuvar tamatu vāḻkkiyi naṭatti iṟivaṉ tiruvaṭi nīḻaleytiṉāreṉṟu koḷvatē ivaratu iṟutipaṟṟik kūṟappaṭum nikaḻccikaḷiṉ uṇmik karuttākum.
10. piṟa nūlkaḷ
‘tūṅkiṉavaṉ tuṭiyil tirittatu kayiṟu’ eṉṟāṟ pōla, tiruvaḷḷuvarpērāl ñāṉaveṭṭiyāṉ, tiruvaḷḷuvar ñāṉam eṉpaṉa mutaliya ciṟu nūlkaḷ āṅkāṅkuk kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟiṟkum tirukkuṟaṭkum ettuṇip poruntāmi
yuṇṭō, attuṇip poruntāmi avarkkum an nūl
kaḷukkum uṇṭeṉpatu kaṇṭu aṟivuṭiyulakam amintoḻuku
kiṉṟatu vāḻka tiruvaḷḷuvar! vāḻka tirukkuṟaḷ!!
vāḻka!
“vaḷḷuvaṉ taṉṉi yulakiṉukkē tantu
  vāṉpukaḻ koṇṭa tamiḻ nāṭu”


AN INTRODUCTION TO 
THE STUDY OF 
THIRUVALLUVAR
Foreword
T.Sundaram Chettiar, B.Com.,
District Governer,
Lions International Dist, 304 (South) Maduri.

It will not be an exaggeration if we say that, of all the great poets,philosophers and thinkers of the ancient as well as modern times,  Thiruvalluvar stands foremost. But it is a matter for regret that his immortal work, Thirukkural, perhaps the greatest and noblest lliterary creation of the human intellect ever produced, is not so much known to the outside world as those of Homer or Virgil, Plato or Aristotle, Dante or Goethe and Shakespeare or Milton.
	Even in India, beyond the boundaries of Tamil Nad, the knowledge and study of Thirukkural is deplorably meagre and though it far exceeds in its grand and noble conception of the philosophy of life, both material and spiritual, and in its scientific treatment of the four branches, viz., Aram, Porul, Inbham and Veedu, it has not become as much popular as Ghita or Upanishads.
	The reason for this sorry state of affairs is not far to seek.  Despite the fact that Thirukkural, according to one of its titles - Podhumari - is connon to all nations, all religions and acceptable for all times, it still remins a sealed book and a forbidden fruit to many of the Indians and to the foreigner.  We have to blame ourselves for this becuse, while people of other countries, why even our brethren like Bengalis, are propagating their language and literature throughout the world, we are vinly indulging in our glorious past.
Yet it is gratifying to note that attempts are now being made by Tamil scholars, educationsl and other cultural bodies to spread the knowledge of Thirukkural among the peoples of the world and it is being translated in different languages by competent authors.  I am particularly  glad that one of such attempt by my esteemed and learned friend Professor Avvi S.Duriswami Pilli has resulted in the form of a paper entitled as “An Introduction To The Study of Thiruvalluvar” which has been now brought out in a book form with a view to help the non-Tamils to have access to the greatest, most valuable and inexhustible treasure - Thirukkural.  While congratulating Professor Avvi S.Duriswami Pilli of his achievement, I wish that he and other Scholars of Tamil should offer many more such treatises to non-Tamils especially to Europeans and Americans.
T.SUNDARAM.

AN INTRODUCTION TO THE STUDY OF  THIRUVALLUVAR
Mr. President, Ladies and Gentlemen,
I have great pleasure in expressing at the outset my grateful thanks for the kind opportunity, you, the Lions of Maduri, have given me to address you on Thiruvalluvar.  This opportunity is noteworthy for the reason that our Union Government has recognised the greatness of Thiruvalluvar and has issued Postal Stamps with his image.
The present trend of thiking among the educated is quite different from that of those in my younger days; the outlook of our young men now is broad and international.  It looks upon man as man; his colour, caste and creed are ignored.  The uplift of man, as Tolstoy puts it, lies in the endeavour to rise the super element of human aspect and curb and control “the animal man” in him.
It is therefore proper and legitimate the the Lions club of Maduri takes up for its study Thiruvalluvar whose object and aim of study centre in and around the human society, Valluvar, you know, lived in Mduri when the Egyptian and Athenian civilizations were at zenith.  Great thinkers and philosophers like Plato, Aristotle and a host of others were shining bright and influencing the human society to a greater extent.
Ancient Grecian writers inform us of the import of Tugi from Indies; Thiruvalluvar also points out the export of the same tugi in one of his couplets.
1.	pīlipey cākāṭum acciṟum appaṇṭam
   	cāla mikuttup peyiṉ
Our Bharat was not wanting in thinkers like Thirualluvar in those days.  There were Bodhayana, Apasthamba.  Asvalayana, Koutilya and Parasara, besides Manu, the law giver.  But the mind of these thinkers was so saturated with communal feelings called Varnashrama Dharma that they could not extricate themselves from bias.  They thought and did every thing in terms of caste and community.  Even the author of the famous  Bhagavat Gita, who devotes a separate chapter to the castes and the respective communal duties and Birthru Hari, the author of Neethi Sathagam,  are not free from this mental distemper.
That is why Prof. Sundaram Pilli went to the extent of saying.
2. 	vaḷḷuvarcey tirukkuṟaḷi maṟuvaṟa naṉku 				uṇarntōrkaḷ
 	uḷḷuvarō maṉuvāti orukulattukku orunīti
i.e if anybody studies Thirukkural in detil he will never like the code of Manu and Smirities which mete out justice on the basis of caste and community.
From these we can see that Thiruvalluvar is the only great thinker superior to those law givers and moralists mentioned before and his views are in tune with the modern sociological trends which tend to a formation of a casteless and classless community, free from all types of distinctions by virtue of birth or practice. Sublimation of life by following the natural rules of conduct is the sole aim of Tirukkural and it does not place any religious or political faith or thought, as the be-all and the end-all of life.  It is in pursuance of this aim that Valluvar takes the whole human society into consideration and wants to establish it on the bed-rock of moral virtue.  The unit of society according to him consists of husband and wife.   If the man and woman of a family - unit are well qualified to lead a virtuous life, the whole of an ideal society would enjoy permanent peace and pleasure.
In the introductory chapters of Tirukkural,  human society is viewed both materially and morally.  In the first chapter.  Thiruvalluvar explins the importance of prayer to God as the only solution for the scares and anxieties and pangs of birth and death, the ambitious longing for wealth and pleasure And the last, if not the least,  the innate ingnorance which is responsible for all the deeds, good or bad,
The drama of human life is to be enacted at the face of the earth.  The material aspect of  life  rests upon the food-stuff, which is the immediate product of the rin; but just like the grace of God,  rin is also beyond the control of man. 
The existence of worldly life is  dependent upon the rin; it is the only thing responsible to ensure living  for all beings. Thiruvalluvar has thought very minutely of the importance  of  the rin and declares that even though the earth is surrounded by expansive seas and oceans;   it will be as useless as the poet says, “ Water water everywhere, not a drop to drink.’’   Modern science can provide us with distilled water when our water cans go dry in the midst of the sea, but this artifical distilled water is tasteless and hence incapable of quenching our thirst; that is why thiruvalluvar says that even the watery oceans will shrink in their importance, if there is no rin at all.
3. 	neṭuṅkaṭalum taṉṉīrmi kuṉṟum taṭintueḻili
   	tāṉnalkā tāki viṭiṉ
Man mintins his power of elevating his human spirit to super human and divine level simply becuse his physical actvities are crowned with pleasure and plenty on account of the rin.  Consequently  penance and patience help him to rise his spiritual existence up and above ordinary human level.
4. 	tāṉam tavamiraṇṭum taṅkā viyaṉulakam
  	vāṉam vaḻaṅkā teṉiṉ.
Even when the human society, by the grace of Lord God and the heavenly rin, is provided with peace and plenty, its worldly social life has to be governed by the most experienced and wise men of keen intellect and broad spiritual outlook.   They are gifted with keen insight so that with the philosophic process of analysis and synthesis they are able to analyse the universe into five and more elements mentally and appreciate psychologically the inner working of each element.  This is put in a nutshell as.
5. 	cuvi yoḷi ūṟuōci nāṟṟameṉṟintiṉ
	vakiterivāṉ kaṇṇatē ulaku.
Having lived their life most profitably both for others and for themselves, they take to asectic life and devote their time to give pieces of advice to the experienced and guide the inexperienced. 
The ascetic life they had adopted gives ample opportunities for introspection and self negation and ripens their mind to render all possible help to their fellow beings.  That is why their actions are considered to be the greatest which no other human being can do.  This our Valluvar says:
6. 	ceyaṟkariya ceyvār periyar ciṟiyar
   	ceyaṟkariya ceykalā tār.
They are embodiments of love and sympathy.  They are called antaṇar!(Andhanar)  becuse their love is towards the welfare of all living beings.  As such, they become the accredited leaders of the human society.  Every word they utter is held as law for human conduct.   Their words and ideas of virtues, morals, laws and regulations are incorporated in this word kural.
In the last chapter of the Introductory part.  Thiruvalluvar gives the general scope of the aṟam (Aram) which he wants to present to us in the following chapters.
Aram in Tamil is a very comprehensive term and it includes all virtues, morals, laws both individual and social and regulations of conduct both in the private and public life.  The first and foremost object of any individual is to attin glory and obtin wealth.  He desires that his life graph must always be stright and on the positive side i.e. plus side, and so Thiruvalluvar says to him that this is possible and attinable if Aram is adhered to strictly and followed most faithfully.
Man’s actions, good or bad, are first conceived in the mind and then put into action.  Aram can be worth doing only when it is conceived in the mind which is pure.  Therefore the first requisite he enjoins on us is to be pure in mind.
7.	maṉattukkaṇ mācuila ṉātal aṉittu aṟaṉ
 	ākula nīra piṟa.
In putting this Aram into action one must be careful in eschewing uncharitable envy, illegitimate desire, unscrupulous anger and mischievous offensive speech.  The purpose of man’s life on earth is to attin pleasure;  no power or pelf is capable of bringing lasting peace and pleasure; history tells us in its true verasity, that sceptre and  crown have tumbled down and rich diadems have been thrown away rejected on the ground that they were incapable of yielding happiness with peace.  Man’s burning desire is to attin unalloyed, untroubled and everlasting pleasure and peace.  Even this is not impossible provided yo are an ardent devotee of Aram; Aram is the only source of that everlasting pleasure and nothing else.
8.	aṟattāṉ varuvatē iṉpammaṟṟu ellām 
  	puṟatta pukaḻum ila.    
PART - I
Gentlemen.
	Having introduced Aram in this way Thiruvalluvar begins to enlarge his subject of Aram and exhorts the human society to follow it.
In the beginning he begins with the house hold life becuse according to Valluvar and his predecessors as we have already seen family is the unit of the human society.  But our modern politicians advocate individuals as the unit of the soceity and they base all the Governmental activities taking the individual male or female as the unit.  According to our traditions and culture family-unit is indivisable and sacred.  Splitting family into individuals has torpeded peace and tranquility at home.  This reduces the house to a temporary lodging for the male and the female individuals.  There is another school of thought professing the village community as the unit of the human society.  Here, the unit is heterogeneous in its structure and the active are absent on account os their exodus to urban areas.  Thus we can see how realistic our Valluvar is and true to our culture; he gives a lead to the entire human community.
The domestic life of each family or the unit of the human society is replete with duties and services to be rendered to the community at large.   Human life on earth is always interdependent; physical, economical, social and intellectual inder-dependance is the summum bonum of life.  With this in his view, Valluvar says that every house-holder must be of help,  assistance, and guidance to his fellowmen besides being a minstay of the ascetics, poor and needy, and those who are too old and feeble to fend for themselves.  Ancestral worship, devotion to duty, hospitality to the guests and timely help to relatives are the general duties imposed upon each householder.  In discharging these duties, the male and female of the family-unit must on no account forget to preserve their person by attending to its needs and comforts.  It is the human body which is the most indispensable instrument of carrying out the various Arams; the essence of existence.  The wife of the house-holder is called the life-partner and she occupies the supreme position of the family in its internal affairs.  In the days of Valluvar their union was not separable by any law or regulation.  No government had the right to cut asunder their partnership in life.  The government of the family was entirely in the hands of the wife and her family budget never ran into deficit.  She was the home and finance minister to the government of the family.
	Every duty of the family is discharged in consanance with the dignity of the house.  And the domestic expenditure is in proportion to its income.  The name and fame of the family rests entirely in the virtuous conduct of the wife.  Among the most glorious things on earth, nothing is superior to a woman who upholds her chastity with firmness of mind.
9.   peṇṇiṟ peruntakka yāvuḷa kaṟpeṉṉum
     tiṇmiyuṇ ṭākap peṟiṉ
To her the loving husband is the Lord God, and bowing low at his feet she controls the entire elements of nature, and comands them to execute her wish.  Valluvar says that rins will pour down if she desires.  She is vigilent in guarding herself and her chastity, always attentive to her husband’s comfort, ever anxious to safeguard the tradition and dignity of the family and unfailing in the discharge of her domestic duties.  It is her sincere conviction that her husband, her life partner, attins everlasting glory and bliss. She will, in turn, without any doubt be deified in the world where gods live.
10.   peṟṟāṉ peṟiṟpeṟuvar peṇṭir peruñciṟappup 
       puttēḷir vāḻum ulaku.
	She therefore directs her thought, word and deed towards rising the right reputation of her husband in the paths of domestic virtue.
Any lacking in this aspect of her life will not only drive the whole family to docks but her poor husband will lose his manly appearance, lion-like walk, before his scorning foes.
11.   pukaḻpurinta illilōrkku illi ikaḻvārmuṉ
      ēṟupōl pīṭu naṭi.
From these we come to the conclusion that a virtuous wife is a real blessing to a house.  But this is not sufficient.  She must  bring forth brilliant children.  They are fine ornaments to the household.  She is not only responsible for the prevalence of peace and pleasure but should beautify the household life by bringing up good children.  It must be clearly understood that procreating children is not the sole purpose ot the family life; according to Valluvar, Tholkappiar and other ancient Tamil sints, the childen were true ornaments as well as wealth to the family and to that effect they were brought up.
Of all the valuable acquisitions a husband and wife of a family can have, nothing is greater than begetting intelligent children.  Sons and dughters of unimpeachable character will enable their parents to enjoy all pleasure not only in this life but in the seven-fold births they may take in future and that is becuse great thinkers of the past declared one’s children as one’s fortune.  The wishes and the best thing that a father can do to his children is to educate them to such an extent as would elevate them to the forefront of the councils of the wise.
The parents feel that, by the education they give, if their sons and dughters possess for higher degree of knowledge than themselves, it would do immense good to all beings at large.  When the society recognises the greatness of her son and offers him the foremost of its place, the mother’s heart is filled with pride and happiness exceedingly greater than what she enjoyed when she gave birth to him.  Having shown the true extent of the parental care and love towards the intellectual growth of the son, Valluvar exhorts the son that the best requittal that a greatful son could offer to his father is to make the wise men prise the parent for presenting so noble and virtuous a son for the benefit of the human society.  With this chapter Valluvar completes the structure of the unit of the human society.
These family-units are bound together by the sacred bond of genuine love; this love is spontaneous and nothing can obstruct it from its flow.  From love springs affection to all and that in turn results in real friendship with others
12.aṉpuīṉum ārva muṭimi atuīṉum
	naṇpueṉṉum nāṭāc ciṟappu!
This genuine, spontaneous flow of love not only encourages virtue but is of utmost importance to avoid and remove evil from the minds of the wicked and cruel.  The very existence of the living beings is due to this love; but for  this love, the whole universe will be devoid of entire beings and it will be full of skin-clad bones.  We thus understand that love is nothing but a binding force pulling all the members together to form a solid human entity called family.  This love bound family comes in contact with the other units and by mutual love, these units of the human society are linked and knit together.  Thus the life of the human beings becomes the movement of love, affection and friendship
In 17 chapters, Valluvar deals with the positive and negative characteristics of the working of the family units along with the whole human society.  Instead of entering into the detils, let me confine myself to the presentation of the general scope for your thought at present.
Reception and cordial entertinment of guests and other fellowmen, utterance of kind and sincerely sweet words, gratefulness to people who are of help, assistance and guidance and sense of uprightness and equity in transactions are the min positive elements for success in family life Of course, experience of our life on earth tell us that the course is not smooth.  There are some negative forces arising out of our movement in the society at large.  Failure to control our mind and speech, covetuousness, lack of forbearance and fortitude, evil tendency of enjoying others’ possessions and back-biting, indulgence in frivolous and vin talks are the most important negative characteristics which impede the pursuit of the house-holder to achieve his object.  In order to overcome these anti-social negative elements, Valluvar points out the importance of the generosity of mind, liberality of help to others without any thought of recompense, feeding the hungry and helping the poor and needy.
The ultimate fruit of a life devoted to the various duties already enunciated is earthly name and fair renown; otherwise life is not worth living.  As regards the reception of people, Valluvar says that the love that has hitherto been confined within the family - husband, wife and child - begins to manifest itself when a guest arrives to the family.  No matter whether the guest is a stranger or not, he is received with courtesy and affection.  The householder thinks that the sole object of running a family is to receive and serve others whether they are already known or otherwise.
13. iruntu ōmpi ilvāḻva tellām viruntōmpi 		    vēḷāṇmi ceytaṟ poruṭṭu
In order to achieve this object every individual must speak sweet and kind words.  Only such words are declared sweet which come out from  righteous persons with heart full of love and speech completely guileless.
14. iṉcolāl īram aḷiip paṭiṟuilavām
	cemporuḷ kaṇṭārvāyc col
Speaking always kind and pleasant words born of humility, not only serves as an ornament but protectes one from falling a prey to indigence which produces misery.
15. tuṉpuṟūum tuvvāmi illākum yārmaṭṭum
	iṉpuṟūum  iṉcola varkku.
It helps every man to mintin friendly relation with all.  The next allied Aram that brings an individual into the fold of the society is his gratefulness to the help and assistance he receives from others.  As every human being is interdependent, helping and being helped by others are not infrequent.  Some of the helps received might have been done without any expectation of return, some may be in times of need and some might have been without any thought of its high value.  Even when the help done is as small as millet’s seed, wise men who realise the fruits of the act, will only think of the benefit derived by such help and be grateful.  The true value of the help received does not lie in its quantity; but it depends entirely upon the worth and quality of the person who receives it.
16.utavi varittaṉṟu utavi utavi
	ceyappaṭṭār cālpiṉ varittu
The quality of being grateful is quite essential for the proper working of the human society; it helps us to wipe out from our memory any wrong done to us however deadly it may be
17.koṉṟaṉṉa iṉṉā ceyiṉum avar ceyta
	oṉṟunaṉ ṟuḷḷak keṭum
The next factor that makes an individual is indispensable to the society is the sense of uprightness or equity.  Man is apt to be biased on account of various interests in the midst of which he lives and moves.  Sense of equity and uprightness must be above all kinds of bias and partiality.  Opportunities to swerve from uprightness are greater in earning wealth as the comforts of worldly life depend more upon it and neglect of equity and justice also becomes necessary in protecting the ill - gotten wealth.  Therefore, Valluvar averes that wealth acquired by proper and fair means never  dwindles away but passes safely to the posterity.
18.ceppam uṭiyavaṉ ākkam citiviṉṟi
	eccattiku ēmāp puṭittu
If a slight deviation from justice were to fetch you the most maximum amount of wealth and power. Valluvar strongly condemns it saying that  it must be rejected with cotemptuous disregard instantaneously.
19.naṉṟē tariṉum naṭuvikantuām  ākkatti
	aṉṟē oḻiya viṭal
i.e. Give up all wealth however good and pleasant it may be, if it is acquired by dubious means.  Trade and Commerce will flourish only when people engaged in it are upright in their dealings.  Unjust.  and unfair transactions in trade will result in the fall of the society in the estimation of other peoples of the world.
20. vāṇikam ceyvārkku vāṇikam, pēṇip
	 piṟavum tamapōl ceyiṉ.
Supposing a businessman or a judge in a court of law entertins a thought which failsto equipoise his mind, then he must take it for granted, that it is a sure sign of disaster and destruction awaiting to befall himself and the nation he belongs to.
21.keṭuvalyāṉ eṉpatu aṟikataṉ neñcam
	naṭuvorīi alla ceyiṉ
In the routine of our life, especially when we come in contact with men and matters highly beneficial, there is every likelihood for our mind to go beyond our control, and our words more excessive.  Self-restrint or control is the only weapon to guard us from consequential downfall.
22. kākka poruḷā aṭakkatti ākkam
	 ataṉiṉū ṅkilli uyirkku
Of couse, self control is excellent in all, but it most befits those whom fortune smiles upon.  If the mind sometimes break the control, it will begin to roam about in thougt and its harm will not be so serious as the unrestrined word; when it loses its control over the tongue, words become harsh and hot and cuse grievous wounds to others.  Thiruvalluvar says:
23. tīyiṉāl cuṭṭapuṇ uḷḷāṟum māṟātē
	  nāviṉāl cuṭṭa vaṭu.
i.e. ‘The wound may heal though from a burning brand, And be forgotton; but the wound never heals a burning tongue inflicts.’ (Ellis).  Even though a person may be unrestrined in all else, he must have effective control over his tongue; otherwise he will be degraded by licentious speech and put to trouble by his indiscreet words.  Control of mind and restrint over tongue of an individual contribute a good deal to the well being of the human society.
Then comes the conduct and discipine of each householder.  He must conform to the general discipline and decency the society requires.  He cannot isolate himself from the social structure and its laws.  It he fails to understand, in spite of all his extensive learning, that he is a part and parcel of the society and that, as is the society so he must move, he will be looked down upon as one devoid of any refined culture.  This conformity to social requirements is called “oḻukkam”(Olukkam) in Tamil by Valluvar.  Adherence to Olukkam rises one to the exalted place in life and therefore it must be protected well and prized above one’s life.
24. oḻukkam viḻuppam taralāl oḻukkam
	  uyiriṉum ōmpap paṭum
Proper observance of the social  conduct will be considered good, and will be crowned with peace plenty and pleasure; if wrong course is adopted the person will be undone for ever.
25. oḻukkam uṭimi kuṭimi iḻukkam 
	  iḻinta piṟappāy viṭum
The best mark of a person having Olukkam (oḻukkam) is that he will never utter evil words even in the moment of forgetfulness.
26. oḻukka muṭiyavarkku ollāvē tīya 
	  vaḻukkiyum vāyāl colal.
In moving about in the human society, the householder has ample opportunities to come across men and women belonging to other families.  Man’s attention is arrested, and desire kindled, when beauty confronts him.  The observing mind may be influenced and try to covet the thing of beauty; if it is the person of a female, the degree of its pull may be great.  Here, you see that the success of the householder lies in controlling his mind.  If the householder is really a wise man, he will at once overrule his mind that the particular woman is the sole property of another individual over whom he has no clim. No Dharma Sastra or Artha Sastra pronounces any such clim.  Even if the husband of the particular lady is such a close friend that he will never suspect if anything untoward takes place, the fellow who commits the outrage will be counted as living dead.  Close friendship, high and influential position in life, economic and physical strength - these things intoxicate fools to  tresspass others’ houses.  According to Thiruvalluvar, any other sin commited by any individual may be tolerated but not adultery, as he condemns it as the mother of all sins.
Another thing that upsets the mind of the disciplined householder is the insult inflicted by others.  One can face any amount of prise; but it will be too unbearable for one to receive insults.  Having been exasperated with undueinsults, if a householder begins to retaliate, he loses his self-control and descends to the level of the person who insulted him either out of ignorance or foolishness.  If a person wants to keep always his mind subservient to his will, he should guard his patience very zealously.  If at all any man resorts to retaliation,  it will fetch him a satisfaction which will be momentary; but if he forgives the evildoer, all glory and honour will be then to him throughout the ages the world lives.  Encouraged by the pride of immense wealth and exceeding valour, if the miscreant commits insult after insult, forbearance is the only weapon to conquer him decisively.  Amo ng the unpleasant words heard, those which cuse insult are capable of destroying the peace of mind.  Even ascetics of high order lose their control and punish the evildoers.  If a householder bears with patience the insolent words thrown at him, he will be far greater than those noble ascetics.
27. uṇṇātu nōṟpār periyar piṟarcollum
	  iṉṉāc col nōṟpāriṉ piṉ
i.e.ascetics who fast and practise yoga are of course great; but any person who puts up with the insults is greater than those ascetics.  Hence it will be found that forbearance is another factor which binds an individual with the society and is a source of mental tranquility.  In the life of a householder, the wealth and high status of his neighbour or fellowman may kindle the fiery emotions of envy and bring ruins to his household.  A well disciplined and virtuous house holder will therefore instead of envying others feel immensely pleased with the wealth of his fellowmen.  He thinks that true virtue called Olukkam (oḻukkam) is to be free from envy and that oḻukkam in its turn gives Viluppam (viḻuppam) i.e. the highest moral satus in the society.
28. oḻukkāṟāk koḷka oruvaṉtaṉ neñcattu
     taḻukkā ṟilāti iyalpu.
29. viḻuppēṟṟiṉ aḵtu oppatuilliyār māṭṭum
	  aḻukkāṟṟiṉ aṉmi peṟiṉ
If, by mistake, any householder allows his mind to be usurped by the evil tendency of envying others, he will at once try to break the common law and covet wealth by iniquitious means.  The blind covetousness will in turn drive him to complete destruction.
30 iṟal īṉum eṇṇātu veḵkiṉ viṟalīṉum
	 vēṇṭami eṉṉum cerukku
A covetous mind instigates one to conspire aginst his neighbour and derive cruel pleasure at the fall cused by his Envy.  Some fall a prey to this inhuman crime simply becuse they do not possess the particular object of their covetous desire.  at times a highly educated individual, even when he is placed above wants, becomes illegally covetous and does great havoc to his neighbour.
Another characteristic of certin anti-social elements is indecent back-biting, which is worse than covetousness.  There is a section of people who have made it their profession to speak ill of others; this at times carries them to the extent of black - miling .  But it must be understood that  this defect is not incorrigible.
